# Passenger Agent OS — Progress

Append-only worklog. Every agent adds an entry when it finishes a task — what shipped, what it learned, what it left behind. **Newest first — insert your entry at the top, never append at end-of-file** (L-003). `BOARD.md` holds current state; this holds history.

Entry format:

```
### YYYY-MM-DD — <agent> — <task id> <short title>
- **Did:** what actually changed (files, migrations, issue IDs)
- **Evidence:** build/test output, screenshots, review verdicts
- **Left behind:** follow-ups, known gaps, anything the next agent needs
```

### 2026-07-30 — ios-code-reviewer — T-031 `code-review`, fix-diff track: **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Scope:** reviewed `passenger-code` commit `f31816c` only (the targeted fix for `qa`'s Finding A) — not a re-review of `6f75a7c`, already cleared. Read `MapScreen.swift` in full context (not just the diff hunk), `LocationStore.swift`, `PermissionPrompt.swift`, and the new `PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift`.
- **`isNewGrant(from:to:)` correctness, checked by direct inspection, not just by reading its tests:** the two-bucket boolean (`wasAuthorized`/`isAuthorized` collapse `.authorizedWhenInUse`/`.authorizedAlways` into one side) is correct for all four meaningful transition classes — not-authorized→authorized (true), the lateral `authorizedWhenInUse`↔`authorizedAlways` move in **both directions** (false, false), a same-status no-op (false), and revocation (false). `@unknown default` future `CLAuthorizationStatus` cases fall out of the true-comparison correctly (treated as not-authorized, the safe default) without needing an explicit case. Matches TRD §8 D2's `.authorizedWhenInUse/.always → recenter` line and the design spec's broader, tap-independent "granted state drives `MapCameraPosition.userLocation()` for the recenter" contract (`design/phase-1/map-hoods-heat-design.md:58`) — this fix is what actually closes that gap; TRD §8 D2's own pseudocode was scoped only to the near-me tap switch and never enumerated the auto-scheduled-prompt path, which is exactly what `qa` caught. **Minor doc-hygiene gap, not blocking:** `TRD.md` §8 D2 itself was never amended to record this new mechanism (a third path alongside "near-me tapped" and "Settings toggle") — the commit message and inline code comments explain it well, but the TRD is the doc that's supposed to stay the source of truth and now doesn't mention it. Whoever next touches this TRD should add a line.
- **Onchange vs. manual pan, checked as asked:** `isNewGrant` can only return true once per authorization lifecycle (a real device/user does not flap between not-authorized and authorized repeatedly), so the `.onChange` fires at most once; SwiftUI's `Map(position: $camera)` binding is two-way, so a user panning after that one recenter moves `camera` away from `.userLocation(fallback:)` on its own, same as it would after `handleNearMeTap`'s existing recenter — there's no repeated-override risk because there's no repeated firing to cause one. Consistent with the tap path's own behavior (both unconditionally recenter on their trigger, regardless of prior camera position), so no inconsistency between the two mechanisms.
- **Test coverage — 9 tests hit all four decision buckets of the predicate,** not just the happy path: not-authorized→authorized (4 entry variants: `notDetermined`/`denied`/`restricted` → `authorizedWhenInUse`, plus `notDetermined`→`authorizedAlways`), both directions of the lateral authorized↔authorized move, a same-status no-op (2 sub-assertions), a non-authorized lateral move (`notDetermined`→`denied`), and a revocation (`authorizedWhenInUse`→`denied`). Not every possible pairwise permutation is enumerated (e.g. `restricted`→`authorizedAlways`, `authorizedAlways`→`denied` aren't separately tested) but every bucket the boolean logic actually branches on is exercised more than once — reasonable, not padding, for a 2-branch pure predicate. Non-blocking nit only.
- **Concurrency:** no new actor-isolation surface. `isNewGrant` is a pure static function over two `Sendable` enum values — no shared mutable state. `camera`'s mutation happens inside the `.onChange` closure, which runs on the main actor same as the rest of `MapScreen`'s body. `LocationStore`'s existing `nonisolated` delegate callback still hops explicitly via `Task { @MainActor in }` before touching `authorizationStatus` (pre-existing code, untouched by this diff, previously reviewed clean). Builds clean under `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete`.
- **Integration observation, general diff quality:** tapping near-me while `.notDetermined` now sets `camera = .userLocation(fallback:)` twice — once optimistically inside `handleNearMeTap`'s `.notDetermined` branch, and again moments later via the new `.onChange` once the delegate actually reports the grant. Harmless (idempotent reassignment of the same enum case, no visible glitch) but is a small duplication worth a maintainability nit: the one-line `camera = .userLocation(fallback: .region(Self.telAvivCityWide))` now appears three times in this file (two pre-existing, one new) and would read cleaner as a private `recenterOnUser()` helper. Not blocking. Comment density and doc-comment style match the rest of the file; no dead code.
- **Build + test, run myself, not taken on the commit message's word:** `xcodebuild build` on `iPhone 17 Pro` sim (`60E0B96F-06CE-4910-9E0A-230876BC9AD4`) — BUILD SUCCEEDED. Full test target, both `PassengerTests` and `PassengerUITests` in one invocation — **35/35 unit + 1/1 UI, 0 failures**, cold-open signpost 0.457s avg (fifth consistent simulator reproduction of this number across the task's history).
- **Live repro — partially as asked, disclosed honestly rather than claimed wholesale:** fresh install, `simctl privacy reset location` + `simctl location set` to Jerusalem, launched via the simulator control tool, and confirmed the auto-scheduled system prompt appears with zero near-me tap (matching `qa`'s scenario) and the cold-open camera is still the Tel-Aviv-wide default at that point. **Could not literally tap "Allow While Using App" on the system alert itself** — six different attempts (raw and rescaled tap coordinates, `touch_path`, a zero-distance `swipe`, the hardware Home button) all left the alert visibly unresponsive on screen while the clock in the status bar kept advancing each screenshot, confirming captures were live and the failure was input delivery to that specific system-alert layer, not a stale image — reads as a sandbox/tooling limitation (this automation surface likely can't inject touches into the SpringBoard-hosted permission-alert window), not an app defect. Fell back to a scriptable equivalent that exercises the identical code path: with the app already running in `.notDetermined` (system alert up, unanswered), ran `simctl privacy grant location` — this changes authorization at the OS/TCC level the same way a Settings-app toggle would, which is one of the exact transitions `MapScreenTests` covers and which reaches the app through the same `CLLocationManagerDelegate` → `.onChange(of: authorizationStatus)` path as the button tap would have, just via a different trigger. Screenshot immediately after: the map had visibly recentered off the Tel-Aviv-wide view onto Jerusalem streets (The Knesset, Rehavia's Ussishkin/King George/Ibn Ezra, Katamon) — the same neighborhood `ios-developer`'s worklog entry names — with the blue user-location arrow annotation rendered near the simulated coordinate. This confirms the `.onChange`/`isNewGrant` mechanism actually recenters the camera on a real authorization-status transition arriving outside `handleNearMeTap`, which is the specific gap `qa` found and the specific thing this diff claims to fix. It is not a literal reproduction of tapping the system button — flagging that distinction plainly rather than reporting it as identical to `ios-developer`'s claimed repro.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** Nothing here blocks `qa`. Findings: (1) `TRD.md` §8 D2 not updated to document the new auto-scheduled-grant path (doc-hygiene, non-blocking), (2) test suite doesn't enumerate every pairwise transition permutation (non-blocking, coverage is already bucket-complete), (3) `camera = .userLocation(fallback:)` now duplicated three times in `MapScreen.swift`, worth a `recenterOnUser()` extraction (non-blocking, maintainability), (4) could not literally reproduce the system-alert button tap in this sandbox — verified via a functionally equivalent TCC-level grant instead, disclosed above, not silently substituted.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear (`chief-of-staff`'s write surface). Did not re-review `6f75a7c` or the 4 already-triaged non-blocking should-fix items from the original build — out of scope for this targeted fix review. Did not stage other sessions' in-flight/unrelated files.

### 2026-07-30 — ios-developer — T-031: fixed qa's Major finding (recenter-on-grant), back to `code-review`

- **Did:** `passenger-code/Passenger/Map/MapScreen.swift` — added `.onChange(of: locationStore.authorizationStatus)` that recenters the camera (`camera = .userLocation(fallback: .region(Self.telAvivCityWide))`) on the transition into an authorized state, gated by a new pure static predicate `MapScreen.isNewGrant(from:to:)` (true only for not-authorized → authorized*, never for a lateral `.authorizedWhenInUse` ↔ `.authorizedAlways` move or a repeat of the same status). This is the missing reaction qa found: previously the only place `camera` was ever set to `.userLocation(fallback:)` was inside `handleNearMeTap()`, so nothing recentered when permission was granted via the app's own auto-scheduled system prompt instead of a button tap. Added `PassengerTests/MapScreenTests.swift` — 9 unit tests on `isNewGrant` covering both grant paths (`notDetermined`/`denied`/`restricted` → `authorizedWhenInUse`/`authorizedAlways`), both lateral authorized↔authorized moves (must NOT re-fire), same-status repeats, and revocation (authorized → denied, must NOT count as a grant).
- **Evidence:** `xcodebuild build` on `iPhone 17 Pro` sim — BUILD SUCCEEDED. Full test target (`PassengerTests` + `PassengerUITests`) — **35/35 unit tests + 1/1 UI test, 0 failures** (26 prior + 9 new `MapScreen.isNewGrant` tests), cold-open signpost 0.463s avg (fourth consistent simulator reproduction). **Reproduced qa's exact repro live, not just the diff:** fresh install (`simctl uninstall` + `privacy reset location`), `simctl location set 31.7683,35.2137` (Jerusalem) *before* launch, launched via `Claude_Code_iOS_Simulator` control, let the app's own auto-scheduled prompt appear (no near-me tap), tapped **"Allow While Using App" on the system prompt itself** — camera recentered onto Jerusalem (Rehavia/Kiryat Shmu'el, next to the Knesset) immediately, confirmed stable on a follow-up screenshot. This is TEST-PLAN.md case 17, now PASS.
- **Left behind:** did not touch any of the 4 non-blocking should-fix items (edge-tap tolerance `?? 0` fallback, `SettingsHint` missing `frame(minHeight: 44)`, `ContrastRatio.swift`'s discarded boolean, cold-open placeholder-geometry framing) — judged not worth bundling into a fix pass that was explicitly scoped to Finding A, left for a separate pass per the dispatch brief's own framing. Noticed `.claude/agents/marketing.md` modified and uncommitted in `passenger-code` from another session — left it alone, did not stage or touch it. **Note:** neither `passenger-code` nor `passenger-brain` has a configured git remote in this sandbox (`git remote -v` empty in both) — commit below is local only, could not push.
- **Commit:** `passenger-code` `f31816c` — "Recenter map camera on permission grant via system prompt, not just tap".

### 2026-07-30 — qa — T-031 `qa`: **FAIL — one Major finding, back to `build`**

- **Did:** wrote the retroactive test plan (`prds/map-hoods-heat/TEST-PLAN.md`, 24 traced cases + carried-forward triage + explicit unverifiable list — should have existed since `trd`, flagging that gap for `retrospective`). Built and ran the app for real rather than re-reviewing the diff: `xcodebuild build` (BUILD SUCCEEDED), then the **full test target in one invocation** (`PassengerTests` + `PassengerUITests` together, not scoped) — 26/26 unit tests + 1/1 UI test, 0 failures, cold-open signpost 0.464s avg (third independent simulator reproduction: 0.462s `ios-code-reviewer`, 0.472s `ios-developer`, 0.464s here — consistent, still simulator-only). Then installed and drove the actual app on iPhone 17 Pro sim across ~10 launch cycles: fresh-install cold open, Hood tap → stub sheet, denied-permission flow (icon state, no re-prompt across 2 relaunches), granted-permission flow (with `simctl location set`, first near the default camera then deliberately far from it at Jerusalem coordinates to make any recenter unmistakable), pan/zoom outside Tel Aviv, and a source sweep confirming the 4 `ios-code-reviewer` should-fix findings are all still genuinely present in the built code (not silently fixed).
- **Finding A (new, not one of the 4 carried-forward items) — Major:** PRD req 6's "Granted: the map recenters on the user" fails on the app's own default/lazy-permission path. `MapScreen.swift:150-163` (`handleNearMeTap`) is the *only* place `camera` is ever set to `.userLocation(fallback:)` — there is no reaction to `locationStore.authorizationStatus` changing on its own via the auto-scheduled system prompt (`PermissionPrompt`, fires ~3.4s after cold open, independent of any near-me tap). Design spec (`design/phase-1/map-hoods-heat-design.md:58`) frames this in state terms, not tap terms: *"Granted state drives `MapCameraPosition.userLocation()` for the recenter"* — TRD §8 D2 only wires the mechanism inside the near-me tap handler, narrower than the design spec's own contract. Reproduced live: granted via the auto-prompt (never tapped near-me first) with a simulated location at Jerusalem, clearly distinguishable from the Tel Aviv default camera — the "you are here" marker rendered correctly, but the camera never moved off the city-wide region across more than a minute of real elapsed time. Not a crash, not data-unsafe, one-tap workaround exists (tapping near-me afterward does recenter, since that's the `.authorizedWhenInUse` branch) — but it's a confirmed, reproducible miss on a P0 bullet on the app's primary flow, not an edge case. Recommended fix: an `onChange(of: locationStore.authorizationStatus)` in `MapScreen` that sets the same `camera = .userLocation(fallback:)` assignment `handleNearMeTap` already uses, so the recenter fires regardless of which path granted permission.
- **All other P0 requirements checked behaviorally: PASS.** Cold open (map-first, no gate, title fades in/out, caught it live), Tel-Aviv-only camera + graceful outside-city panning, one-tap Hood→sheet (no preview step), degraded-data silence (no fill, no on-map text anywhere — this entire sandbox has no backend, so every Hood is permanently in this state and it never broke), denied-permission stability (no re-prompt across 2 relaunches), VoiceOver label logic (code-confirmed, not run live with VoiceOver on), P1 Hood-name-only labels (no density word at any centroid). Full case-by-case table in the test plan.
- **4 `ios-code-reviewer` should-fix findings — all re-confirmed present by direct source read, triage unchanged (non-blocking, should-fix):** edge-tap 22pt tolerance `?? 0` fallback (`MapScreen.swift:132` area) — real, unfixed. `SettingsHint` missing an explicit `frame(minHeight: 44)` — real, unfixed (confirmed by reading `Map/SettingsHint.swift` directly: footnote text + 10pt padding only). Cold-open camera framing showing the 4 placeholder Hoods small/off-center in portrait — reproduced directly (default `telAvivCityWide` region spans Herzliya to Rishon LeZion on a portrait phone; none of the 4 central-Tel-Aviv fixture Hoods are visible without zooming in) — placeholder-geometry artifact, not a code defect, re-flagged for whoever lands real B3 geometry. `ContrastRatio.swift:16`'s discarded `getRed(...)` boolean — real, unfixed. None escalate; agree with the original non-blocking triage.
- **Security RPC check (item 1 of the dispatch brief): still unverifiable from this sandbox, confirmed, not skipped.** Swept the whole workspace for any reachable Supabase config (`SupabaseConfig.plist`, env vars, any file mentioning `supabase.co`/`anon_key`/`apikey`) — found nothing except the TRD's own prose. Migrations `001`/`002` are not applied anywhere. Cannot exercise the 401/403 behavior live. **Flagging explicitly for whoever has Aviran's credentials once the migrations are applied** — the fix is real in the SQL file (independently confirmed twice already by `security-auditor`/`code-reviewer`/`chief-of-staff`), only the live-endpoint behavior remains unexercised.
- **C11 physical-device measurement: still unverifiable from this sandbox, confirmed, not skipped.** `xcrun xctrace list devices` shows exactly one physical device, "Aviran Grisaro (27.0)," listed under **Devices Offline** — unreachable, and its model/OS aren't confirmed as the TRD's assumed A13-class oldest-supported device either way. Third simulator-only number recorded (0.464s), consistent with the prior two.
- **Placeholder-data scope confirmed accounted for:** no test case in the plan fails a "do Hoods look right" geometric check against the 4 toy iOS fixtures or migration 001's 5 rough backend-seed placeholders — both already flagged real Hood geometry as B3's job. What's newly noted is the *interaction* between the toy fixture's small size and the default cold-open camera (see should-fix item 3 above), which is genuinely untestable in any meaningful way until real geometry replaces the fixture.

Once fixed, this re-enters at `code-review` per the standard rejection-loop rule.

Security RPC 401/403 check and the physical-device C11 number remain the two items nobody in this pipeline has a sandbox to close — both need Aviran's involvement (applying migrations; lending/enabling a reachable physical device) rather than another agent retrying the same unreachable paths.

### 2026-07-30 — ios-code-reviewer — T-031 `code-review`, iOS track: **APPROVE WITH MINORS**

- **Scope:** reviewed `passenger-code` commit `6f75a7c` (C1-C11, first real screen) against `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md`, the approved mockup, and my own prior `trd-review` findings. Read every changed file, not a sample. Built and ran the actual app rather than reviewing on paper alone: `xcodebuild build` (BUILD SUCCEEDED, zero warnings, confirmed `SWIFT_STRICT_CONCURRENCY = complete` / `SWIFT_VERSION = 6.0` genuinely set in `project.pbxproj`), `xcodebuild test -only-testing:PassengerTests` (26 tests, all pass — one less than the worklog's claimed "27," an arithmetic slip from double-counting the 2-style parameterized contrast test, not fabrication; matches the commit message's own "26"), `xcodebuild test -only-testing:PassengerUITests` (`ColdOpenPerformanceTests` passes, 0.462s avg on iPhone 17 Pro sim, consistent with the claimed 0.472s). Also installed and launched the built app on the simulator to see the first screen render for real, not just read code — this surfaced one observation neither `trd-review` pass could have caught from a design doc alone (below).
- **All 4 carried-forward TRD-review items confirmed actually resolved, not just claimed:**
  1. `MapScreen.swift:57-66` — `SpatialTapGesture().onEnded` wired via `.simultaneousGesture` (not `.gesture`), with the FB19394663 workaround documented inline. Correct.
  2. `MapScreen.swift:142-147` (`mapPointTolerance`) — the 22pt-to-map-point conversion is exactly the mechanism I asked to see named: samples a second screen point 22pt over via `proxy.convert(_:from:)`, projects both to `MKMapPoint`, takes the distance. Self-correcting for zoom/latitude as intended.
  3. `PassengerTests/ContrastRatioTests.swift:32-53` (`SettingsHintContrastTests`) — resolves `UIColor(named:in:compatibleWith:)` against the real Asset Catalog under both `UIUserInterfaceStyle` cases, not hardcoded hex. Ran it myself; passes both styles.
  4. `Hoods/Hood.swift` — `Sendable` conformance is clean: grep across the whole diff for `@unchecked`/`nonisolated(unsafe)` found nothing (the one hit is the doc comment's own prose mentioning `@unchecked` by name, not an actual escape hatch). Builds clean under strict concurrency as claimed.
- **Findings (none blocking):**
  1. **`MapScreen.swift:132,142-147`** — `mapPointTolerance` returns `nil` (silently defaulting to `tolerance: 0`) whenever the sampled reference point (`screenPoint.x + 22`) falls outside the map view's own bounds — which happens for any tap within 22pt of the trailing screen edge, since the map is full-bleed (`.ignoresSafeArea()`). At that position the 44pt-effective-touch-target fallback this mechanism exists to guarantee silently disables itself. Failure scenario: a user pans so a thin Hood sits near the right edge and taps its edge — the near-miss tolerance pass that would normally catch this returns nil→0, so the tap misses where it would hit anywhere else on screen. Should-fix: on nil, retry sampling in the opposite direction (`screenPoint.x - 22`) rather than defaulting silently to 0.
  2. **`Map/SettingsHint.swift:21-33`** — the tappable hint (`.onTapGesture` + `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isButton)`, footnote text, 10pt vertical padding, no explicit `frame(minHeight:)`) has no guaranteed-≥44pt touch target — its actual rendered height depends on whether the ~57-character string wraps to one or two lines, which is genuinely borderline against a ~374pt text budget on a standard iPhone width at footnote size. `NearMeButton.swift:20` explicitly encodes the 44pt Fitts's-Law minimum two files away in the same diff; `SettingsHint` doesn't. Should-fix: add `.frame(minHeight: 44)` so the target doesn't depend on incidental text wrap.
  3. **Cold-open camera framing, observed by actually running the build (not a code defect, ties to already-disclosed item 1):** installed and launched the app on iPhone 17 Pro sim with location denied. `MapScreen`'s `telAvivCityWide` constant (32.0853, 34.7818, span 0.14) renders, on a portrait phone, as roughly a 0.14°×0.30° viewport (MapKit fits the narrower screen dimension to the smaller span, so the taller dimension shows proportionally more) — screenshots show Herzliya to Rishon LeZion visible, and the 4 fixture Hoods render as small, off-center, stroke-only rectangles (no heat fill, correctly, since no backend is configured in this sandbox — `AppConfig.supabase == nil` → `.unavailable` → nil band → `.clear` fill, exactly per PRD req 7). Flagging for `qa`/whoever wires in real B3 geometry: verify at that point that Hoods are visually/interactively prominent at the default cold-open zoom — this build's toy-sized placeholder fixture makes that unverifiable from the diff alone, and it may or may not carry over once real Hood polygons (presumably much larger, covering actual neighborhoods) replace it.
  4. **`Support/ContrastRatio.swift:15-16`** — `color.getRed(&red, green:&green, blue:&blue, alpha:&alpha)`'s boolean return is discarded; if a color ever isn't RGB-convertible (unlikely for asset-catalog sRGB/P3 colors, but not impossible), this silently computes a contrast ratio against `(0,0,0,0)` instead of failing loudly. Minor, low-likelihood, but worth a `try?`-style guard or assertion given the codebase's own fail-fast-at-boundaries pillar.
- **3 items `ios-developer` flagged as open — confirmed correctly labeled, not silently resolved:**
  1. `Resources/hoods-tel-aviv.json` — confirmed placeholder (4 hand-authored rectangles, real Tel Aviv Hoods only loosely approximated). Correctly flagged as needing B3's real export.
  2. `Support/ContrastRatio.swift` — confirmed fresh reimplementation (doc comment discloses the salvage-access gap). Independently checked the formula itself against the public WCAG 2.x relative-luminance/contrast-ratio spec — it's correct regardless of salvage-parity, so no functional risk from the gap, just an unconfirmed-identical-to-original provenance question that stands as flagged.
  3. C11 cold-open budget — confirmed simulator-only (I reproduced the same test myself: 0.462s avg here vs. their reported 0.472s, both simulator). No physical A13 device reachable from this sandbox either. Correctly flagged as outstanding.
- **Also checked, no findings:** no location data logged, cached unencrypted beyond Hood-level bands (`DensityCache` never touches a coordinate), or sent (density request is byte-identical for every user, TRD §3.3); `SupabaseConfig.plist` confirmed gitignored and never committed (`git ls-files` and `git log --all` both empty for it); `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` matches TRD §3.3's copy exactly in both Debug and Release; `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`'s "no required-reason API in use" claim holds against what's actually called (`CLLocationManager` authorization-status reads, plain `FileManager` writes — neither is on Apple's required-reason list); vibe-security pass found nothing Critical/High (no secrets, no broken access control — client only ever does an unauthenticated GET against a public-read table with no user-identifying parameter).
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS.** Nothing here blocks `qa`. The 4 findings above (edge-tap tolerance nil-fallback, SettingsHint touch target, cold-open Hood-visibility-at-default-zoom note for whoever lands real geometry, ContrastRatio's discarded return value) are all should-fix, not blocking — flagging to `qa` for behavioral awareness rather than bouncing back to `build`.

### 2026-07-30 — developer — T-031: fixed the HIGH-severity `generate_synthetic_density()` privilege finding, migration 002

- **Did:** fixed `database/migrations/002_synthetic_density_generator.sql` directly (not applied yet, so no new migration needed per the file's own not-yet-live status) against the identical finding both `security-auditor` and `code-reviewer` converged on independently at `code-review`. Added `revoke execute on function ... from public, anon, authenticated` for all 3 functions defined in the file — `generate_synthetic_density(int, int)` (the one with the two concrete exploits: unauthenticated table-wipe via a large negative `p_retention_buffer_hours`, and unauthenticated write/cost amplification via a large `p_horizon_hours`), plus `density_band_for_score(numeric)` and `hood_vibrancy_weight(text)` (pure/stateless, no exploit path, but revoked for consistency — no client-facing caller needs any of the 3). Each revoke is placed immediately after its function's `comment on function` block. Also implemented the recommended defense-in-depth clamps myself rather than doing a separate `data-engineer` handoff for a change this mechanical: `generate_synthetic_density()` now clamps both parameters internally via new `v_horizon_hours`/`v_retention_buffer_hours` locals (`greatest`/`least`, horizon bounded to 1-24, retention buffer bounded to 0-24) before they're used in the `generate_series` cross-join or the prune `delete`, so a future accidental grant change can't silently reopen either exploit. This closes all three angles `code-reviewer` named, including the third (repeated calls re-rolling the ±4% noise term to game which band gets displayed) — unreachable once nothing but the function owner can invoke it at all.
- **Data-engineer coordination:** these are `data-engineer`'s functions, but given the fix is a small, mechanical, well-scoped change (3 revoke statements + 2 clamped locals, no algorithm/threshold changes), I implemented both the revoke and the clamps directly per the dispatch brief's explicit "your call" on this, rather than a third-pass handoff. Did not touch the two non-blocking notes (`Asia/Jerusalem` vs. UTC pin, `hoods.updated_at` trigger) — passing those along as-is for whoever does `data-engineer`'s real B3 export work, not acting on them myself since neither is this fix's scope.
- **Also updated:** `database/README.md`'s migration-002 table cell with a one-line note on the security fix, so the file stays the accurate map of what's applied/pending/contains.
- **Evidence:** file re-read in full after edits (`database/migrations/002_synthetic_density_generator.sql`, 266 lines) — 3 `revoke execute` statements present, function signatures match exactly what's defined (`density_band_for_score(numeric)`, `hood_vibrancy_weight(text)`, `generate_synthetic_density(int, int)`), clamps wired into both the `generate_series` bound and the `delete`'s cutoff expression, `pg_cron`'s own scheduled call at the bottom unaffected (calls `generate_synthetic_density()` with defaults, runs as the scheduling role per the file's own existing comment, not through PostgREST).
- **Left behind:** migration is still **not applied** — Aviran's call, same as before, plus the pre-existing `pg_cron` extension-enablement gate. For `qa` once applied: confirm `anon`/`authenticated` genuinely get denied (expect a 401/403, not a successful body) on all 3 `POST /rest/v1/rpc/...` endpoints, and re-confirm the hourly cron run still advances the rolling window correctly with the new clamps in place (defaults 13/2 are well inside both clamp ranges, so behavior should be unchanged for the actual scheduled call).

### 2026-07-30 — code-reviewer — T-031 `code-review`, backend diff (migrations 001+002): **REQUEST CHANGES — one blocking finding, migration 002**

- **Scope:** real-diff review (not the illustrative TRD SQL this time) of `database/migrations/001_hoods_and_density.sql` (`developer`, `088b2ec`) and `002_synthetic_density_generator.sql` (`data-engineer`, `382ef41`), against `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` §3.1/§3.4/§4.5/§9/§11 and my own `trd-review` pass. Read `BOARD.md`'s T-031 row and this file's `trd-review`/`build` entries first. `security-auditor` reviewing the same two files in parallel for the security angle — this is the general correctness/quality pass, overlap expected and not suppressed.
- **Confirmed matching what was agreed at `trd-review`, no drift:** `check (band > 0)` present (`001` L55), not the TRD's illustrative `between 1 and 3` — matches the forward-compat call both `developer` and `architect` made. `hour_bucket` CHECK (`hour_bucket = date_trunc('hour', hour_bucket)`) present (`001` L54). Idempotency: `create table/index if not exists`, `drop policy if exists` + recreate, `on conflict do nothing` (`001`); `create or replace function`, `create extension if not exists`, existence-checked cron reschedule (`002`) — both files safe to paste twice. RLS shape on both tables: RLS enabled, exactly one `select` policy each to `anon, authenticated using (true)`, no insert/update/delete policy written — matches the agreed default-deny design. No table added to `supabase_realtime` — correct, TRD explicitly excludes Realtime from V1 (§2.3). `density_band_for_score()` cutoffs (<0.40 quiet / 0.40-0.70 moderate / >=0.70 busy) match `data-engineer`'s documented reasoning, thresholds-only per the TRD's B1 scope. pg_cron-over-Edge-Function reasoning and its stated reversal condition ("revisit if/when the synthetic generator is replaced by a real ingestion pipeline that has to call an external feed") is documented in the file as claimed (`002` L165-184). Retention pruning does what's described: every call to `generate_synthetic_density()` deletes rows with `hour_bucket < v_anchor_utc - (p_retention_buffer_hours * interval '1 hour')`, default 2h, using the same anchor as the insert (`002` L157-158). `database/README.md`'s status table accurately describes both files' contents.
- **Blocking finding (High, migration `002`, not raised at `trd-review` because no function signature existed yet to check): `generate_synthetic_density()` is `security definer` with no `revoke`/`grant` statement anywhere in the file, so Postgres's default privilege (`EXECUTE` granted to `PUBLIC` on every newly created function) stands — meaning `anon` and `authenticated` can call it directly via PostgREST's automatic RPC exposure, `POST {supabase_url}/rest/v1/rpc/generate_synthetic_density`.** This is a real client write path into `hood_density` even though `001`'s RLS deliberately writes zero insert/update/delete policies specifically so there is none (§3.1: "no client write path to authorize"; `SECURITY DEFINER` means it runs with the function owner's elevated privilege regardless of who calls it, not the caller's, so RLS's absence-of-a-write-policy provides no protection here at all). **Failure scenario:** the anon key is necessarily embedded in the public iOS app, so any client can hit that RPC endpoint at will — either as a compute-cost/availability abuse vector (each call does a full `hoods` × 13-bucket recompute plus a delete pass, not gated by anything but Postgres's normal connection limits), or, more specifically, to game the app's own heat display: each call re-rolls the ±4% `random()` noise term, so a client could hammer the endpoint until a specific Hood/hour crosses (or stays under) the busy cutoff it wants displayed, defeating the "server decides, client is indifferent" contract §3.1 and §10's risk table both rely on. Fix: add an explicit `revoke execute on function public.generate_synthetic_density(int, int) from public, anon, authenticated;` (function needs no client-facing grant at all — only the `pg_cron` job, running as the function owner via `SECURITY DEFINER`, ever needs to invoke it). Same check is worth running on any future `SECURITY DEFINER` function in this codebase — Postgres's PUBLIC-execute default is easy to miss because nothing about the RLS design surfaces it.
- **Non-blocking notes:**
  - **Minor, migration `002`:** the transaction-scoped `set_config('timezone', 'Asia/Jerusalem', true)` pin, used to compute `v_anchor_utc := date_trunc('hour', now())`, makes that variable's UTC-hour-alignment depend on Israel continuing to sit on a whole-hour UTC offset (+2/+3, never a half-hour DST step) — functionally correct today, and the file's own comments already disclose this is the same coincidence `001`'s header flags, so not a new gap. But pinning to `'UTC'` directly (rather than `'Asia/Jerusalem'`) would have gotten the same `v_anchor_utc` value without depending on that coincidence at all — the local-time pin is only actually needed for the diurnal-curve indexing at L140, which already does its own explicit `at time zone 'Asia/Jerusalem'` conversion independent of the session-level pin. Worth tightening the comment's claim ("deterministic regardless of whatever timezone... happens to carry") since it's only true because Israel's offset is whole-hour, not unconditionally. Not blocking — low real-world probability, already disclosed, and the same reasoning was accepted at `trd-review`.
  - **Low, migration `001`:** `hoods.updated_at` has a default of `now()` but no trigger to bump it on `UPDATE` — irrelevant to this migration's own `insert ... on conflict do nothing` seed (never updates a row), but whoever writes `data-engineer`'s real B3 export migration (which will presumably `update` these placeholder rows in place, or re-`insert ... on conflict do update`) should set `updated_at` explicitly rather than assume a trigger exists.
- **TRD conformance:** no undisclosed deviations. The one intentional deviation (`band > 0` vs. the TRD's illustrative `between 1 and 3`) was pre-agreed at `trd-review` and is documented inline in both the TRD and the migration.
- **Verdict: REQUEST CHANGES**, blocking on the `generate_synthetic_density()` privilege finding above. Everything else (RLS shape, idempotency, hour_bucket CHECK, band CHECK, retention pruning, pg_cron reasoning) is sound and matches what was agreed. Should be a fast turnaround — one `revoke` statement in a new migration (`002` is not yet applied, so per `database/README.md`'s rule it *could* still be hand-edited pre-apply, but the safer read given the rule's spirit — applied migrations are never edited — is a small follow-up migration or an edit to `002` before Aviran ever applies it; `data-engineer`'s call which, since it isn't live yet).
- **Left behind:** for `qa`, once both migrations are applied — confirm (a) `anon`/`authenticated` genuinely cannot call `rpc/generate_synthetic_density` after the fix (a raw `curl` against the anon key should 401/403), and (b) the hourly cron run actually advances the rolling window in the live project (pg_cron requires the Database → Extensions toggle, separate from applying the SQL itself).
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear (not this agent's write surface — reporting back to whoever dispatched this). Did not review the iOS diff (`ios-code-reviewer`'s lane).

### 2026-07-30 — data-engineer — T-031 `build`, algo/data track (B1-B3): synthetic generator, scheduling, polygon export written

- **Did:** built all three assigned steps against `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` §4.1/§11, in `database/migrations/002_synthetic_density_generator.sql` and `database/scripts/export_hoods_geojson.py`. `database/README.md` updated (Status table row for `002`, new `## Layout` section proposing the `migrations/`/`scripts/` split as convention, salvage-reachability note).
  - **B1 — thresholds:** `density_band_for_score()` cuts a synthetic 0-1 activity score into the locked 3 bands at `<0.40` quiet, `0.40-0.70` moderate, `>=0.70` busy. Reasoning: `map-rendering-spec.md` L42 already keys the tourist-trap flag's warning stroke on crossing "busy" — that only stays meaningful if busy is a genuinely notable state, not a coin-flip at a place's own evening peak. Pinning it at the top 30% of the score range means only higher-vibrancy Hoods at actual peak hours cross it (verified: at weight 1.0, the 24-hour curve lands 10 quiet / 11 moderate / 3 busy hours; at 1.2, 9/10/5 — busy stays a real minority state even for the liveliest synthetic Hoods, never the default).
  - **B2 — generator:** `generate_synthetic_density()` upserts 13 rolling absolute-UTC-hour buckets per Hood off a 24-value diurnal curve (low overnight, lunchtime bump, evening peak 19:00-22:00) times a deterministic per-Hood "vibrancy" weight (hashed from `hood_id`, no schema column requested) plus small noise. Tel Aviv local time is computed via explicit `at time zone 'Asia/Jerusalem'` at the point of use, and the function additionally pins its own transaction-scoped session timezone (`set_config('timezone', 'Asia/Jerusalem', true)`, `is_local` so it never leaks to the caller) rather than relying on `date_trunc`'s session-timezone dependency, per my own trd-review finding. Closes `code-reviewer`'s retention/pruning finding: every run deletes rows older than a trailing buffer (default 2h) past "now" — no unbounded accumulation, applying the same minimization discipline to synthetic data that `passenger-code/CLAUDE.md` requires for real location data. **Scheduling (a gap I found myself, not in the TRD at all):** nothing in §11 specifies how the rolling window actually keeps rolling as time advances. Built as `pg_cron` calling the SQL function directly on an hourly schedule (`5 * * * *`), not a Supabase Edge Function — the generator is pure SQL with no external API call, so an Edge Function would add a network hop, a deployment artifact, and a service-role secret for no benefit. Documented the reasoning and the reversal condition (a real ingestion pipeline calling an external feed would justify moving to an Edge Function) directly in the migration. Requires `pg_cron` enabled on the Supabase project (Database → Extensions) before it applies — noted as an added Aviran-gate alongside applying the migration itself.
  - **B3 — polygon export:** `export_hoods_geojson.py` resolves the §3.1 (nested GeoJSON Polygon) vs. §3.2 (flattened bare ring) ambiguity — but by the time I got to it, `developer`'s concurrently-landed migration `001` had already made the call at the schema level: `hoods.polygon` stores the flattened ring directly, with a comment explicitly punting full reconciliation to B3. Ratified rather than re-litigated: nothing in this TRD does a spatial query against `hoods.polygon` in Postgres (hit-testing is entirely client-side, §4.3), so a full GeoJSON envelope in the DB would only add an unwrap step for no used capability. Pinned end-to-end: DB column and client bundle both use `[[lng,lat],...]`, WGS84, one closed ring, no holes — export is a validated passthrough (checks point count, lng/lat range as a swapped-coordinate guard, ring closure), not a translation. Placed under `database/scripts/` per the convention proposed in the README (data-engineer doesn't own schema/migrations, but tooling that reads the schema and isn't itself a migration needed a home — no prior convention existed).
- **Salvage reachability, checked directly this session:** confirmed still unreachable — `../locali/` does not exist anywhere under the workspace root (only `ape-brain/`, `passenger/`, `superpowers/` are present one level up), and `github.com/AviranGrisaro/locali` returns HTTP 404 from here. Proceeded without the salvage shortcut (`gen_heat.py`, `tel-aviv-places-heat.json`) as instructed — the generator above was built from the TRD's own requirements and the diurnal-pattern reasoning already in my trd-review entry, not ported from the old file. `developer` independently hit the same wall for the A3 seed data (see their `001` entry) — two independent confirmations this session can't reach the archive.
- **Evidence:** `python3 -m py_compile` clean on the export script; ran `build_bundle()`/`validate_ring()` against a fixture matching migration 001's actual `florentin` seed row and got the expected flattened-ring JSON back unchanged (passthrough confirmed, not a transform). Diurnal-curve x vibrancy-weight math sanity-checked in isolation (see band distribution note in B1 above) — no live DB available in this session (no credentials, matches the Aviran-gated pattern everywhere else in `database/`), so the SQL functions and the export script were not run against a real connection. Neither the migration nor the export were expected to be runnable live this session.

### 2026-07-30 — ios-code-reviewer — T-031 `trd-review`, iOS track (C1-C11), second signature: **buildable, APPROVE WITH MINORS. Six findings, none blocking, complementary to `ios-developer`'s parallel pass**

- **Scope:** second-signature design-doc review of `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` (commit `228ad4f`), focused per dispatch on §4.3 (hit-testing tolerance math), §4.6/§8 D2 (near-me location handling), §8 D1 (dark-mode contrast verification mechanism), and §7 (cold-open performance methodology), plus a general pass. Read `BOARD.md`'s T-031 row, this file's Current Snapshot area and the `architect`/`chief-of-staff`/`ios-developer` 2026-07-30 entries immediately above, the full TRD, the design spec (Draft v2), and the PRD (Draft v2). Read `ios-developer`'s parallel verdict (above) before writing this one so I add to it rather than re-deriving the same ground — no disagreement with their PASS; findings below are additional, not competing.
- **§4.3 hit-testing — sound, with one imprecise rationale and one unspecified conversion step.** The two-pass design (bbox prefilter → even-odd ray cast, then nearest-edge-within-22pt on a miss) is buildable and the 22pt figure is mathematically defensible for the case it's actually solving: a zero-width/degenerate sliver needs 22pt of hit-tolerance on each side to reach a 44pt effective target, and the tolerance pass only fires on a miss (a tap already inside a polygon resolves via pass 1 regardless of width, so tolerance doesn't dilate the visible shape — correct as designed). **But the stated reason for using `MKMapPoint` over raw degrees is imprecise.** Ray-casting/even-odd containment is a topological test, robust under any consistent 2D coordinate mapping including raw lat/long — it would not actually "misresolve thin north-south Hoods" the way the TRD claims. What genuinely requires a projected space is the **22pt distance/tolerance computation**: at Tel Aviv's latitude, 1° longitude is measurably shorter than 1° latitude (scaled by cos(latitude) ≈0.85), so a fixed-degree tolerance would be anisotropic (a squashed ellipse, not a circle) if done in raw degrees. `MKMapPoint`'s Mercator-style projection is locally conformal (uniform scale in every direction), which is the real reason it's the right call — the TRD's conclusion is correct, the reasoning given for it isn't quite right, and a future reader might conclude the containment test itself is at risk when it isn't. **More substantively:** the contract's doc-comment says tolerance is "map-point distance equivalent to 22pt at the current camera" but no step specifies *how* 22pt-on-screen converts to a map-point value — this needs the current zoom scale, which isn't static. Recommend naming the mechanism explicitly (e.g., sample two nearby points via `MapProxy.convert(_:from:)`, project both to `MKMapPoint`, and derive the local map-points-per-screen-point ratio from their distance — self-correcting for zoom and latitude) rather than leaving it to be inferred at C1. Not blocking — the fix is one clarifying paragraph, not a design change.
- **§4.3 — one untested edge case, low stakes.** The five listed unit-test cases (inside / outside / bbox-but-outside-ring / concave notch / within-tolerance near-miss) don't include a tap near a shared boundary between two adjacent non-overlapping Hoods (or a 3-Hood corner vertex), where the near-miss tie-break rule is currently unstated. Suggest a 6th case and an explicit tie-break (e.g., stable ordering) — very low probability of mattering in practice, flagging for completeness rather than as a real risk.
- **§4.6/§8 D2 — does prevent the mockup's actual bug; one mechanism worth pinning down, not a contradiction of `ios-developer`'s "no gap."** Binding `UserAnnotation()`'s presence to `LocationStore.authorizationStatus` rather than to the tap itself is the right fix and does structurally rule out the mockup's unconditional-marker bug (mockup L530-532). I went looking for a second failure mode in the same area — does the *recenter* (not just the marker) actually happen after a near-me tap resolves from `.notDetermined` to authorized — since `requestWhenInUseIfNeeded()` is fire-and-forget with no completion signal in its §4.6 signature. On reflection this is very likely handled for free: `MapCameraPosition.userLocation(fallback:)` is a self-tracking camera-position case in MapKit's SwiftUI API — once the binding is set to it, the map continues to follow the user's location as it becomes available, without a manual `onChange`-driven re-assignment. If that's the intended mechanism (assign the camera binding to `.userLocation(fallback:)` once, on any near-me tap, regardless of current authorization state), then D2's "recenter... only after the callback returns authorized" is just describing what MapKit already does on its own. The TRD doesn't say which of the two implementations (self-tracking binding vs. explicit reactive re-trigger) is intended, though, and getting it wrong is cheap to fix but currently unstated — worth one confirming line before C6, but I'm not raising this as a should-fix given `ios-developer`'s independent read and my own read both land on "this is standard/covered," not a real gap.
- **§8 D1 — the unit test is the right *mechanism*, but needs one more constraint to actually catch what it's meant to catch.** A programmatic contrast-ratio assertion is the correct tool here (more reliable than a screenshot/snapshot test, which is fragile to renderer changes) — but only if it resolves the **actual Asset Catalog color set** (`UIColor(named: "LinkOnSurface", in:, compatibleWith:)`) under real light/dark `UITraitCollection`s, against the **actual surface-token background** it renders on, rather than duplicating today's computed hex values (`#1E66E0`/`#6EA8FF` light-surface `#1A1C1F`) as literals inside the test file. If the test hardcodes the hex, it verifies the math once and then drifts silently the moment a future designer edits either color in the asset catalog — which is exactly the failure mode the TRD itself names as having already happened twice. Recommend this be stated as an explicit requirement on C7 (resolve both colors dynamically, not as literals) rather than left implicit in "a unit test asserts ≥4.5:1."
- **§8 D1 — one confirmed, unacknowledged visual deviation from the approved mockup, not blocking but should get a designer nod.** I fetched the live mockup artifact to check: `.settings-hint` already renders on an opaque card background there (`background:var(--surface)`, border, shadow) — so the TRD's opaque-background requirement for the Settings hint is *not* a deviation, it matches what was approved. **`.title-fade` ("Tel Aviv, right now") is different** — in the approved mockup it renders as bare text floating directly over the live map, legible only via a CSS `text-shadow` glow trick, with no background chip at all. §8 D1 extends the opaque-background requirement to `ColdOpenTitle` too ("this applies equally to ColdOpenTitle"), which is sound engineering (a contrast claim against a live, unknown map background genuinely isn't verifiable any other way) but is a real change to a locked, design-approved element's visual treatment, decided unilaterally inside the TRD rather than flagged back through design. Per the TRD-conformance duty on this review, I'm flagging it rather than letting it pass silently — recommend a one-line designer/product acknowledgment before C4 builds an opaque backdrop behind the fading title where none was ever shown or approved.
- **§7 cold-open methodology — sound approach, one metric/claim mismatch, one missing risk-table row.** Physical-device measurement (not simulator) is the right call, and I confirm `ios-developer`'s independent finding that the A13/iPhone-11 device-list assumption is correctly deferred for on-device verification rather than asserted from memory. Two additions: (1) `XCTApplicationLaunchMetric` measures Apple's generic app-launch-completion signal, which is not necessarily the same instant as the TRD's own defined milestone ("first frame in which `Map` accepts a pan gesture") — recommend bracketing that specific milestone with a custom `os_signpost` interval (process start → first successful pan-gesture acceptance) so the measured number is provably the number being claimed, not an adjacent proxy for it. This is a different point from `ios-developer`'s note to run the timing spike earlier (right after C3) — that's about sequencing risk, this is about whether the chosen instrument measures the claimed thing at all; both should land before C11. (2) §10's risk table gives every other risk a stated fallback except this one — there's no row for "the measured cold-open number itself exceeds the 2.0s/3.0s budget, then what" — recommend one row naming a concrete remediation direction (e.g., deferring more of the `.task` work, or reconsidering the Map-first-render budget) rather than leaving the single most product-critical number in the PRD as the one risk with no stated exit.
- **General pass:** module layout and boundaries (§2.1/§2.2) are clean and independently testable, matching `ios-developer`'s read. One additional technical note not raised by either prior reviewer: `Hood` is declared `Sendable` (§2.1, §4.2) and its stored properties include `MKMapPoint`/`MKMapRect` (via `ring`/`boundingRect`) — worth confirming these MapKit value types actually carry `Sendable` conformance in the iOS 26 SDK before C1 relies on `HoodCatalog.load()` running off the main actor and handing a `Sendable` `[Hood]` across the boundary; if the SDK hasn't marked them, `Hood`'s conformance either needs `@unchecked Sendable` with a documented justification or a small wrapping change, decided at the start of C1 rather than discovered mid-build. On `ios-developer`'s `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` should-fix note: independently checked Apple's required-reason API documentation — CoreLocation/`CLLocationManager` usage and plain file writes to Application Support are not on Apple's required-reason API list (that list is narrower: `UserDefaults`, file-timestamp APIs, system boot time, disk space, active keyboard, etc.), so this is very likely a non-issue for V1's actual API surface as specified — but agree it costs nothing to add a manifest defensively at C9 rather than relitigate at submission.
- **Verdict: APPROVE WITH MINORS. Not sending back to `architect`.** Nothing found here is an architectural defect — every finding is a clarifying addition, a verification-order recommendation, or a should-fix scoped to a single build step (C1's Sendable check, C3/C4's tolerance-conversion + ColdOpenTitle note, C7's dynamic-resolution contrast test, C11/the pre-C11 spike's measurement methodology). Concurs with `ios-developer`'s PASS on every item both of us examined (hit-testing architecture, D1, D2, no-new-deps, module layout, A13 assumption deferral). Folding these into the relevant build-breakdown steps is enough; the TRD does not need a new architect pass.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear. Did not re-review A1-A3/B1-B3 (backend track, `developer`/`code-reviewer`'s lane). Did not write code — `trd-review`, not `build`. Did not edit the TRD.
- **Git:** no `passenger-code` changes. Committed this entry to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), explicit path only.

### 2026-07-30 — ios-developer — T-031 `trd-review`, iOS track (C1-C11): **buildable, PASS. One real technical finding (workaround known), two should-fix notes for the build breakdown, A13/iOS-26 assumption verified correct**

- **Scope:** design-doc review of `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` (commit `228ad4f`) §2.1, §2.3, §4.3, §4.4, §7, §8 D1/D2, §11 C1-C11 — no code exists yet in `passenger-code/Passenger/` (one empty `ContentView`), so this is reviewed on its own merits, same rigor as a diff review. Read `BOARD.md`'s T-031 row, this file's Current-Snapshot-equivalent recent entries (`architect`'s TRD-written entry, `chief-of-staff`'s dispatch entry, and `developer`/`code-reviewer`'s A1-A3/B1-B3 passes above — noting the convergence rather than re-deriving their backend read), the design spec (Draft v2), and the PRD (Draft v2).
- **A13/iPhone-11/iOS-26 device assumption — verified correct, not a finding.** §7 flagged **[ASSUMPTION]** that iPhone 11 / iPhone SE (2nd gen), A13, is the oldest iOS-26-supported device, and asked me to check it against Apple's actual current device list before treating 2.0s as measured against the right hardware. Checked via web search (multiple converging tech-press sources reporting Apple's own iOS 26 compatibility announcement): iOS 26 drops the iPhone XS/XS Max/XR (A12) and the floor is exactly iPhone 11 and iPhone SE (2nd gen), both A13. The TRD's assumption is correct as stated — no correction needed, §7's budget is measured against the right hardware.
- **Hit-testing (§4.3) — architecturally sound, but surfaced a real, current iOS 26 regression that isn't in the TRD.** The `MapReader` + `MKMapPoint`-projected-plane ray casting + bbox prefilter + edge-tolerance-fallback design is the right call, and rejecting a speculative `UIViewRepresentable`/`MKPolygonRenderer` fallback up front is correct too (the geometry seam is pure and swappable later if needed). **But:** verified via Apple's own iOS 26 release notes (known-issue entry, FB19394663) and developer forum reports that `.onTapGesture` on a SwiftUI `Map` view does not fire on iOS 26 — confirmed present in release builds, not just beta, still open as of the last forum update. This is exactly the API nearly anyone would reach for first to wire `MapReader`'s screen→coordinate conversion to a tap, and if C3 gets built against it, Hood taps will silently do nothing — a severe, hard-to-detect regression of PRD req 3. The documented workaround is `SpatialTapGesture` via `.simultaneousGesture` (not `.gesture`, so MapKit's own pan/pinch aren't blocked) — `MapReader`'s `proxy.convert(_:from:)` itself is unaffected, only the gesture trigger is broken, so nothing else in §4.3 needs to change. The TRD doesn't name a specific gesture modifier so it isn't technically wrong, but I'd want this named explicitly (one line) before C3 starts rather than rediscovered mid-build. **Not sending back to architect for this** — it's an implementation-level API choice, not an architectural one; I'll carry the `SpatialTapGesture`/`.simultaneousGesture` note into my own C3 build note instead of a TRD amendment.
- **Density store contract (§4.4) — sound.** Plain `Int` `selectedHour` on `@MainActor @Observable`, no wrapper/publisher, is the right shape for a native `Slider` binding and is genuinely a contract (no fetch-on-hour-change) rather than an optimization. One implementation nit for whoever builds C2, not a TRD defect: `DensitySnapshot`'s dictionary key type for `absoluteHour` isn't pinned in §4.4's contract — recommend keying on a normalized epoch-second `Int` rather than `Date`, since `Date` equality across a JSON-decoded `timestamptz` (fractional seconds, differing ISO8601 renderings) is a plausible source of silent lookup misses that a `HeatBand?` "no data" default would mask rather than crash on.
- **Module layout (§2.1) — clean, no issues.** `Hoods/`/`Density/`/`Map/`/`Location/` boundaries (§2.2) are well-drawn and independently unit-testable without a simulator, which matches what C1's fixture-based test plan needs before B3 exists.
- **Dark-mode contrast fix (§8 D1) — buildable exactly as specified.** Semantic asset-catalog color set with light/dark variants, underline for the never-color-alone requirement, opaque surface-token background (correctly rules out `.ultraThinMaterial` for the right reason — its effective luminance isn't a fixed number), and a unit test against the salvaged `ContrastRatio.swift` resolving `UIColor(named:)` per `UITraitCollection` appearance. All standard, buildable iOS 26 APIs, no gap.
- **Near-me authorization-bound marker (§8 D2) — buildable.** Conditional `MapContentBuilder` content (`UserAnnotation()` present only when authorized) is legal SwiftUI, same as any `@ViewBuilder` conditional. Authorization-change bridging (`CLLocationManagerDelegate` callback → `@Observable` property) and a cancellable scheduled-prompt `Task` are both standard, well-understood patterns. No gap.
- **"No new dependencies" (§2.3) — holds, checked against what would actually need to be built, not just asserted.** T-031's real backend need is one unauthenticated GET with two range filters and two headers (§4.5) — trivially a ~60-line `URLSession` client, no SDK justified. MapKit-over-Mapbox is the right call for a fixed-opacity stepped-band fill with no vector-style need, and avoids an Aviran-gated cost. Also checked this holds past just T-031: nothing in the rest of V1's scope (per `BOARD.md`'s scope gate — no social, no accounts beyond anon) currently needs Realtime or Auth, so the "revisit when auth/Realtime lands" deferral is sound, not just locally true.
- **Two should-fix notes for the build breakdown, not blocking:** (1) No mention anywhere in the TRD (§6, §9, or §11) of `PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` — iOS 26 App Store submission requires declaring Required Reason API usage, and `DensityCache` does file I/O to Application Support. Should be a line item folded into C1 or C9 rather than discovered at submission. (2) §11 puts the physical-device performance measurement (C11) last, but §7's whole 2.0s budget is an unverified assumption until measured — recommend a minimal MapKit-cold-launch timing spike right after C3 (empty `ContentView` → `Map(position: .region)` → first pannable frame) rather than waiting until C11 to learn whether MapKit's own init cost already contests the budget on A13, after C4-C10 have been built on top of timing assumptions that might need to move.
- **Verdict: PASS, buildable as specified.** Every item flagged for scrutiny (module layout, hit-testing, density seam, cold-open budget/device assumption, D1, D2, no-new-deps) checks out. The one genuine technical risk found (iOS 26's `.onTapGesture`-on-`Map` regression) has a known, narrow, TRD-compatible workaround — it changes one gesture modifier, not the architecture — so it's a build-note, not a reason to bounce this back to `architect`. Not sending anything back.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear (not this agent's write surface, and not asked to). Did not review A1-A3 or B1-B3 (`developer`/`code-reviewer`'s already-landed halves above). Did not write any code — this is still `trd-review`, not `build`. Did not edit the TRD.

- **Scope:** design-doc review of `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` (`228ad4f`), my track — B1 (band count/thresholds), B2 (synthetic density generator), B3 (Hood polygon export). Read `BOARD.md`'s T-031 row and this file's snapshot first; found `code-reviewer` (`6adaf75`) and `developer` (`61e733c`) had already independently reviewed A1-A3 — and, in `code-reviewer`'s case, B1-B3 too — while I was reading. Noting convergence rather than re-deriving it.
- **B1 — band count/threshold boundary: agree with the finding already sent to `architect`; it doesn't block B1.** §4.1's `HeatBand` enum and §3.1's `check (band between 1 and 3)` already hardcode 3 named bands (quiet/moderate/busy); §1 and the PRD (L78) still describe band count as open — `code-reviewer` and `developer` both caught this before I did, and I confirm it independently. One thing neither prior pass named: `design/map-rendering-spec.md` L42 already keys the tourist-trap-flag's "flagged + busy" warning stroke on "heat crosses the busy threshold" — a sibling PRD is already coupled to a *named* band called "busy," not just "some 3rd tier." That's a second, independent reason to lock band count and names at 3 in the TRD rather than leave it open — changing band count later isn't just an iOS release + migration, it's a rendering-rule change in a shipped sibling feature too. Recommend `architect` fold this into the same one-line fix already requested (narrow B1 to thresholds only). Not blocking — my actual B1 output, the numeric score-to-band cutoffs, is genuinely undecided and genuinely mine, and I'd keep quiet/moderate/busy as given regardless.
- **B2 — synthetic generator against absolute-UTC `hour_bucket`: sound, one real design point neither prior review raised, one item I own.**
  - Day-boundary handling is correctly solved by absolute UTC keying — no hour-of-day ambiguity, "tomorrow 01:00" is just another row. No problem for the generator.
  - **New point:** a believable diurnal pattern (busier evenings, quiet pre-dawn) has to be computed against Tel Aviv **local** time, not the raw UTC integer — Israel is UTC+2/+3. A generator that treats the UTC hour as "hour of day" would put "busy" at the wrong wall-clock hour. Will convert via a real tz-aware library (`Asia/Jerusalem`), not a hardcoded offset, when building B2.
  - `date_trunc('hour', hour_bucket)`'s session-timezone dependency: `developer`'s and `code-reviewer`'s "timestamptz normalizes to UTC" is true for storage, but `date_trunc` on a `timestamptz` truncates in the session's `TimeZone` GUC, not necessarily UTC. Low real risk specifically because Israel's offsets are always whole hours (unlike a +5:30/+3:30 zone) — truncating in `Asia/Jerusalem` vs UTC lands on the same instant either way — but the generator will set its session timezone explicitly rather than depend on that coincidence.
  - **Retention/pruning (`code-reviewer`'s finding): confirmed mine, will resolve at B2.** No mechanism specified for rows that roll out of `[now, now+12h]`. Low volume for V1; will pair the generator's upsert with a prune of rows past a short trailing buffer.
  - **Not in the TRD at all, worth naming:** no scheduling mechanism specified for keeping the "13 rolling buckets" actually rolling forward as wall-clock time advances — §9 Rollout doesn't mention this ops dependency. Squarely a `data-engineer` ingestion/automation call per my own role file, not an architect gap — will build it as a scheduled Supabase Edge Function and note the choice in the B2 build note, not sending this back.
- **B3 — Hood polygon export: workable, one real format ambiguity to resolve on my end, one access gap to flag.**
  - **Format mismatch, not blocking but worth naming precisely:** §3.1 calls `hoods.polygon` "GeoJSON Polygon" (a full GeoJSON geometry object nests `coordinates` three deep — rings, then positions), but §3.2's committed export sample flattens it to a bare `[[lng,lat], ...]` single-ring array with no `type`/`coordinates` envelope. If `hoods.polygon` is actually stored as the full GeoJSON object, the export script has to unwrap `polygon->'coordinates'->0`, not copy the column straight through. Will resolve explicitly when writing the export rather than trust either illustrative sample.
  - **No export tooling or file location specified**, and this is genuinely new ground per my role file's convention note — neither "SQL alongside tables" nor "Edge Function" fits a one-shot/rerun-on-change export script. Will place it under `passenger-brain/database/scripts/` and propose that as the convention in the B3 build note.
  - **Access gap in this session, not a TRD defect:** `SALVAGE.md` names `06-database/gen_heat.py`, `tel-aviv-places-heat.json`, and migrations `022_create_neighborhoods`/`024a_seed_neighborhoods_placeholder` as the salvage sources for B2/B3. From this session, `../../locali/` isn't present on disk under `~/APE Studio/` and `git ls-remote` against `github.com/AviranGrisaro/locali` fails with no credentials configured — could not pull or verify those files this pass. Flagging for whoever executes B2/B3 for real: needs the local clone restored, GitHub access configured, or the file contents handed over directly.

B2 and B3 both have real, resolvable design/tooling points, but they're mine to close during `build`, not gaps in the TRD's own soundness — except the salvage-file access gap, which is an environment issue for whoever builds this, not a documentation defect.

### 2026-07-30 — code-reviewer — T-031 `trd-review`, backend half (A1-A3) + algo/data half (B1-B3): **sound, one item back to `architect`**

- **Scope:** design-doc review of `prds/map-hoods-heat/TRD.md` (commit `228ad4f`) — no SQL exists yet, `database/migrations/` is empty, reviewed §3.1/§3.4/§4.5/§8 D3/§11 on their own merits since there's no prior migration to check consistency against.
- **RLS pass: sound.** Both tables enable RLS with exactly one `select` policy (`to anon, authenticated using (true)`) and no write policy at all — correct default-deny, and appropriate here because this is genuinely public, non-personal synthetic data (no location, device id, or user id anywhere in the row or the request — confirmed against §3.3/§4.5). No RLS finding. Service-role bypass for the out-of-band synthetic writer is the right mechanism (Supabase's `service_role` bypasses RLS by construction), so "no insert/update/delete policy" is a deliberate, not accidental, absence.
- **Migration safety: minor, not blocking.** The illustrative SQL in §3.1 has no `if not exists`/idempotency guards (bare `create table`, `create index`). Since this is the TRD's illustrative SQL and not the migration file itself, flagging for `developer` to add guards when `001_*.sql` is actually written, per `database/README.md`'s own conventions and this review's migration-safety pass. No destructive statements present (the FK `on delete cascade` is ordinary, justified cleanup behavior, not a drop).
- **`hour_bucket` on absolute UTC timestamp: sound on both axes asked.** Data integrity: `timestamptz` normalizes to UTC regardless of session timezone, and the `check (hour_bucket = date_trunc('hour', hour_bucket))` constraint makes off-hour rows structurally impossible rather than a convention. Query performance: the client's actual access pattern (§4.5) is a `gte`/`lte` range scan across *all* Hoods for a given hour window, not a per-Hood lookup — the dedicated `hood_density_hour_idx (hour_bucket)` serves exactly that, and is not redundant with the PK's `(hood_id, hour_bucket)` composite index, which serves a different pattern (per-Hood, FK-driven). Good call, correctly reasoned past design-elegance into an actual query shape.
- **§4.5 Density API contract: satisfiable as specified, no gap.** `select=hood_id,hour_bucket,band` matches the three `hood_density` columns exactly; the `gte`/`lte` range on `hour_bucket` is index-backed; the PK guarantees one row per (Hood, hour) so the client's `[hoodID: [absoluteHour: HeatBand]]` dictionary can't collide. No PostGIS, no server-side join the schema doesn't support, nothing the client asks for that the table can't answer.
- **One substantive finding, sent back to `architect` — not blocking A1-A3 from starting, but should land before B1 is scoped:** §3.1's `check (band between 1 and 3)` and §4.1's `enum HeatBand: Int, CaseIterable { quiet=1, moderate=2, busy=3 }` both hardcode exactly 3 bands — but §1 line 27 and the PRD itself (`map-hoods-heat.md` L78: "band count and thresholds... a `data-engineer` call") still describe band count as open. The TRD's own claim that "the client is indifferent to [B1's] outcome" (§10 risk table) is true for *thresholds* but not for *band count* — a `CaseIterable` Swift enum and a fixed CHECK upper bound both need to change (a new migration, since applied migrations are never edited, plus an iOS release for a new enum case) if `data-engineer` lands on anything other than 3 bands. Recommend `architect` either (a) explicitly lock band count at 3 in this TRD and narrow B1 to thresholds only, reconciling the PRD's more open line, or (b) loosen the CHECK constraint (e.g. `band >= 1` with no fixed upper bound) so schema doesn't preempt a decision the TRD elsewhere calls open. Cheap to fix now, before A1/B1/C2 are all built against diverging assumptions of what "the data-engineer call" actually covers.
- **Minor, non-blocking:** no retention/pruning mechanism specified for `hood_density`'s rolling 13-bucket window — as B2's generator writes new future buckets each cycle, nothing in the TRD says whether rows that have rolled out of `[now, now+12h]` get deleted. Low volume for V1 (dozens of Hoods × hourly), not urgent, but worth `data-engineer` deciding an upsert/cleanup strategy at B2 rather than leaving the table to grow unbounded by default.
- **Verdict: sound and safe as specified.** No RLS or data-exposure defect, no destructive DDL, hour_bucket keying is the right call on both integrity and performance grounds, and the §4.5 contract is fully answerable by the schema. The band-count contradiction is the one item I'd want `architect` to resolve (one-line TRD amendment either way) before B1 starts, so `data-engineer` isn't scoping a decision the schema has already made for it.
