### 2026-07-30 — product — T-033 `design-approval` verdict: **REJECT** (3 blocking, 3 should-fix)

- **Verdict: REJECT.** Back to `design`(designer) per the rejection-loop rule. Three P0 acceptance bullets have no home in the design or are actively contradicted by the published artifact. This is a strong spec otherwise — §2.3's `ui-design-review` pass is genuinely applied, not cited, and both fixes it claims are real in the source — but B1–B3 are exactly the class of quiet requirement-drop this gate exists to catch. `chief-of-staff` has already passed its half; mine does not land, so the gate does not clear.
- **I checked the artifact source, not the spec's description of it.** Fetched the raw HTML/CSS/JS of `06f8a49b-…` and read it end to end. Every finding below cites the mockup's own CSS/JS or the spec's own line, never the relay. Same discipline as the T-031 verdicts, and it is what surfaced B1–B3 — in all three cases the spec asserts the requirement is met and the source shows otherwise.

**Blocking — fix all three, nothing else**

1. **P0-1 "interactive behind the sheet" is asserted, never designed, and the mockup does the opposite.** The spec's entire treatment is one table cell (§2, Hood sheet container): "Map stays visible and interactive behind it (PRD req 1)." No mechanism is named. `.presentationDetents([.medium, .large])` alone does **not** give background interaction in SwiftUI — the default is a dimmed, non-interactive backdrop; it takes `.presentationBackgroundInteraction(.enabled(upThrough: .medium))` or equivalent. The mockup confirms the gap rather than covering it: `.sheet-backdrop{background:rgba(10,10,10,.28)}` with `.show{pointer-events:auto}` and `onclick="closeAll()"` swallows every tap over the map, at both depths. A developer building exactly what §2 says ships a non-interactive map and fails req 1's second bullet. **Fix:** name the mechanism and the detents it holds through; state explicitly whether the Place modal at depth 2 also keeps the map live or deliberately doesn't; make the mockup's backdrop stop intercepting so a reviewer can see the actual behaviour. Note `ux-flows.md` L59 makes the same "map stays 100% interactive/reachable throughout" claim for the search sheet off the same pattern reference — worth getting the mechanism written down once, properly.
2. **P0-7's "state is visible on reopen" has no home anywhere, and the mockup resets it.** `openPlace()` and `openPlaceFromHood()` both execute `state.saved = false;` on every single open, so a saved place reopens unsaved — the exact behaviour the bullet forbids. The spec doesn't cover it either: §2's Save action row specifies only the write path ("writes locally first, instantly and optimistically"), §3's Place-modal state table has Loading / Empty / Error / Offline / Route-unavailable / Permission-denied and **no already-saved row**, and §5's traceability maps P0-7 to that same §2 row, which never addresses reopen. Since the spec's own §2.3 issue-1 fix makes tint-plus-`aria-pressed` the *only* state signal (icon deliberately constant), the already-saved-on-open rendering is precisely the thing the developer needs specified and it isn't. **Fix:** add an "opens already saved" row to §3's table naming what renders (saved tint, border, accessibility label "Saved", no entrance animation on an initial state), and persist saved state per place in the mockup.
3. **P0-4's "≥44pt touch target for every action in the modal" is violated by controls the spec never enumerates.** `.sheet-close{width:32px;height:32px}` — the ✕ dismiss, present on both the Hood sheet and the place modal. `.empty-cta{padding:6px 10px;min-height:32px}` — the "Explore another Hood" button the §2.3 review pass *added*, i.e. the fix introduced a sub-44pt target. §4's "Touch targets" bullet enumerates only Save and Route and stops there. `design/design-principles.md` §2 sets "≥44pt touch targets" with no carve-out for dismiss controls, and `map-rendering-spec.md` §4 already establishes the right pattern for this app — "the tappable area is never just the drawn glyph's bounding box if that's smaller than 44pt" — so a 32pt glyph with a 44pt hit area is fine and no visual change is forced. **Fix:** bring both to ≥44pt effective target, and enumerate *every* interactive control in §4 rather than the two primary ones.

**Should-fix — not blocking on their own, but B1–B3 send this back anyway, so do them in the same pass**

4. **§2.3 and §7 both claim Route's fill is "used nowhere else in either sheet." That is false against the source.** `--primary` is also the saved Save button's icon colour *and* its border (`.save-btn.saved`), sits behind it as `--primary-soft`, and is the empty-state CTA's text colour (`.empty-cta{color:var(--primary)}`). So the moment Save is toggled, the demoted secondary action adopts the primary hue — against req 4's "unique colour" bullet as literally written. The 8x size delta means the hierarchy still reads, so this is a wording-and-token problem, not a broken design: either re-tint the saved state to a non-primary token, or state in §2.3 that "unique" means unique-as-a-fill and reconcile that against req 4 explicitly. What it must not stay is a "checked, not asserted" claim the source contradicts — that phrasing is load-bearing for whoever reads this next. Same paragraph: Route's width is ~322pt, not the "~342pt" claimed (390 − 28 phone-frame padding − 40 sheet padding); the ratio survives, the arithmetic doesn't.
5. **The "Things to do" glyph is a generic map pin (📍), not the landmark glyph §2 says it reuses.** `map-rendering-spec.md` §4 specifies "a fork-and-knife glyph vs. a generic landmark glyph" and warns this is now load-bearing: post-decision-#25 the category glyph is "the *only* way to visually tell categories apart during ordinary browsing… Get this shape/icon distinction wrong and there's no fallback." Fork/knife (🍴) is right; 📍 rendered inside a pin shape is a pin inside a pin and reads as "location," not "thing to do." Cheap fix, and P0-6's "distinguishable by glyph" is weaker than it should be until it's made.
6. **P0-5's "walking mode" disappears after §0.** It appears once, in §0's component list. §2's Route action row, §3's states, §8's open item 2, and §5's traceability row — which paraphrases req 5 and silently drops it — all discuss the hand-off without ever specifying the deep link requests walking directions. That is the one part of req 5 a build can miss without anyone noticing. **Fix:** state it in the Route action row and carry it into §8 item 2 alongside the action-sheet-vs-direct-open question.

**Minors — designer's call, no rework required:** `.closed-note{display:none}` is an empty CSS placeholder for `places-been-saved`'s closed badge, left in the source with a comment; it renders nothing and is harmless, but it's a hook for another PRD's feature and is better deleted than kept. The Hood *button* — req 1's second door — is stated in §1's flow but not demonstrated in the mockup's chrome row (search/heat/profile only); it's T-031's control so that's defensible, worth one line so nobody assumes it was designed here. Casing: `tourist-trap-flag` req 6 writes the string lowercase mid-sentence and the mockup renders "Tourist-heavy spot" sentence-cased at line start — I read that as prose casing in the source PRD, not a string mismatch, and T-035 owns the final treatment either way.

- **What genuinely checks out, verified rather than trusted.** The four deliberate exclusions hold **in the source**, not just in the prose: zero filter chips inside the phone frame (the `.seg` chips are reviewer rail, outside it); exactly one `Route` button with no Scenic/Fast split and no polyline anywhere; no provenance words and no closed badge in the modal; the flag line is one text line plus icon, exact string, `flagged`-state only, tagged `T-035 slot` and annotated as placement-and-string-only. Depth ceiling of 2 is enforced in `openPlace`/`openPlaceFromHood`/`closePlace` and observable via the breadcrumb. §2.3's two claimed fixes are real: `toggleSave()` keeps 🔖 constant across states and flips a genuine `aria-pressed`, and the "Explore another Hood" CTA exists and dismisses (see B3 for its size).
- **I recomputed §2.3's contrast claim rather than accepting it, given T-031's carried-forward dark-mode defect.** All four pairs clear WCAG AA 4.5:1: flag line 6.31:1 light (`#8A5312` on `#FFFFFF`) / 9.16:1 dark (`#F0B429` on `#1A1C1F`); saved Save tint 4.56:1 light (`#1C7A5E` on `#E4F2EC`) / 7.88:1 dark (`#3FD9A6` on `#173028`); Route label on its fill 5.26:1 light / 9.23:1 dark. The light-mode saved tint at 4.56:1 is thin but passes. No repeat of T-031's `--focus` defect — that token is overridden in both dark blocks here.
- **Scope gate clean, re-run from scratch on the artifact source (Job 0 item 4 — inherited work gets the check every time it's touched).** PRD Motivation quotes the authorizing line verbatim and it resolves: `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` L37, "Tap a Hood → hand-curated blurb + tagged spots. Tap a place → detail modal (name, category, save, routing)." Req 5's second bullet quotes L38's "No in-app turn-by-turn, voice, or rerouting in V1." No social surface of any kind in the artifact — no friends, follow, profile, avatar, presence, share, invite, post, sign-in or account. No onboarding, splash, carousel, welcome or tutorial. No Phase 2/3 hooks — no proximity, no shake-to-decide, no persona lens, no AI surface, no stamp/points mechanic. The three chrome-row chips (🔍/🌡️/👤) are shallow stubs carrying `title` attributes naming T-038/T-032/T-037 as their owners, consistent with §0's stub rule; the 👤 Profile entry is decision #39's confirmed gate exception, not an invented surface. **Job 0 item 5 tripwire:** still 8 PRDs against INDEX's "roughly nine to ten." Clean.
- **Job 2 sweep run against all 7 P0s and both P1s, not just §5's table.** P0-2, P0-3 and P0-6 are fully realised in the source and I found nothing to add. P0-1, P0-4 and P0-7 are B1/B3/B2. P1s are correctly handled — hours/photo render-if-present, category grouping openly deferred as "not built into this pass" rather than quietly skipped, which is the honest shape.
- **No PRD amendment this round (L-009 checked and does not apply).** Every requirement B1–B3 failed already had a falsifiable pass/fail bullet — req 1's "visible and interactive," req 4's "≥44pt," req 7's "visible on reopen." The spec defect is in the design, not in the PRD's testability, so there is nothing to write back into `hood-place-detail.md`. PRD stays Draft v1; the L-006 status flip belongs at acceptance, not here.

Two real issues found and fixed in this same pass: (1) the mockup's Save toggle swapped its icon to a checkmark on save, which reads as "confirmed" not "saved" — a meaning swap the manual's toggle-button rule specifically warns against; fixed to keep the glyph constant and communicate state via tint/border plus a real `aria-pressed` attribute. (2) the Hood sheet's "no places curated" empty state had no CTA, reasoned as "nothing actionable" — that doesn't hold up against either the manual's Empty State rule or Passenger's own `design-principles.md` §4, both of which require a CTA as part of the baseline shape, not an optional extra; fixed by adding a real "Explore another Hood" CTA that dismisses the sheet. One trade-off considered and kept, documented rather than silently deviated from: Save stays icon-only despite the manual generally discouraging icon-only buttons outside a short exception list (close/hamburger/search) — kept because it's the ubiquitous native pattern for this exact control in every major map app, mitigated by a persistent accessibility label rather than a visible text label. Also verified numerically, not just asserted: Route vs. Save's size ratio (~8x width, well past the 1.5x minimum), that Route's fill color is used nowhere else in either sheet, and that the flag-line/Save-tint colors clear 4.5:1 in both themes — deliberately checked to avoid repeating T-031's carried-forward dark-mode contrast defect.

### 2026-07-30 — product — third V1 PRD pass: search + quick filters written (the last unblocked V1 item)

- **Verified the brief's premise against source before writing** (Job 0 item 6). Read `strategy/decisions.md` #23/#25/#33/#41 and `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` L36/L47 directly rather than trusting the dispatch. All four hold: #23 puts search in V1 as a sheet reached from map chrome, #25 moved category chips into that sheet, #33 renamed the categories, #41 confirms "quick filters" was Aviran's shorthand for the renamed categories and **not** an instruction to return chips to map chrome — decision #25 stands unreversed. Nothing blocked this PRD.
- **Did — `prds/search-quick-filters/search-quick-filters.md` (Draft v1).** Authorizing line quoted verbatim in Motivation: *"**Search + quick filters**, reached from an icon in the map chrome, opening as a sheet. Covers place names, keywords ("hummus", "rooftop bar"), and Hoods… Results carry the same heat and tag signals as the map and honor the slider hour; search filters the map, it doesn't bypass it."* Second verbatim line for the chips: *"surfaced as **quick filters** inside the search sheet, unchanged from decision #25."*
- **Eight P0s:** sheet-never-a-destination + modal exclusivity; one field matching three result types; the two chips and nothing else as a filter axis; search filters the map at the selected hour (dimming, one dataset, no search-only ranking); a result lands on the same destination sheet a map tap would; every state (empty / no-match / offline / uncurated Hood / location-denied); interrupted-vs-completed state persistence; reach and accessibility.
- **The one thing I deliberately did not resolve — the sheet's layout, and it is Aviran's own ask that is in question.** `design/ux-flows.md` §2 and §9 Q15 record that his original request was a **literal 50/50 top/bottom map-over-list split**, and that design review recommended standard iOS `.medium`/`.large` detents instead, pending his sign-off. That is exactly the L-013 shape — a recorded founder ask with a downstream recommendation sitting on top of it — so I wrote all eight P0s to hold under either layout and flagged it in the header, Not-in-scope, Open questions, and the Decisions log. `ios-developer` must not build against either reading first.
- **One real conflict found between the strategy line and an already-written PRD, resolved as an [ASSUMPTION] rather than a silent call.** Strategy says results "carry the same heat and tag signals as the map"; `tourist-trap-flag` req 6 says the flag lives in the place modal "and nowhere else," and `map-rendering-spec.md` §4 says a pin carries no flag encoding at any zoom. So a per-row tourist-trap line in search results would contradict two locked docs. I read "same signals as the map" as satisfied by the dimmed map underneath plus the destination sheet — which is also what `ux-flows.md` Journey 5 step 3 describes — and labelled it **[ASSUMPTION]** in req 4 with the consequence named: if Aviran means a per-row line, req 4 changes and `tourist-trap-flag` req 6 reopens.
- **Escalation, not a build detail: keyword search has no data behind it.** The strategy line names "hummus" and "rooftop bar" as first-class queries, but `places` carries name, one of exactly two categories, coordinates and `hood_id` — nothing searchable as a keyword, and decisions #11/#33 forbid a third category value. Either the curated dataset gains a keyword/tag field or keyword search degrades to name-matching and the authorizing line goes unmet. In the PRD's Open questions; `data-engineer`/Aviran's call, not `architect`'s to invent.
- **Also flagged, unresolved:** decision #23 makes search a watch-item ("if users reach for search before reading the map, the map isn't working") and nothing in V1 measures that — no analytics pipeline exists. And req 7's interrupted-vs-completed persistence rule is `designer`'s own reconciliation of two decisions made in different sessions (`ux-flows.md` §9 Q17), not a call Aviran made either way.
- **Scope gate clean.** No social surface of any kind — no share, no invite, no profile, no follow. No onboarding, no first-run tips, no search tutorial. No Phase 2/3 hooks: no proximity/distance filter, no shake-to-decide, no persona lens, no AI query surface, no TikTok/Google-Maps import path into results. No third filter axis invented — a "hide tourist-heavy spots" filter is explicitly left as a fresh decision per `ux-flows.md` §6, not quietly specced. **Job 0 item 5 tripwire:** V1 is now 8 PRDs against INDEX's "roughly nine to ten"; each traces to a locked V1 line in `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`. Clean.
- **Self-flagged — word budget, same as the last pass.** 1,555 words against the skill's ~1,200 dense exemption, which I am not self-granting. Two compression passes went in (merged the hour requirement into req 4, cut a Description bullet duplicating req 4, dropped a Thumb-Zone bullet that was unfalsifiable while the layout is open). What is left is 37 falsifiable acceptance bullets, the `Not in scope:` line, three `[ASSUMPTION]` labels and the Decisions log — all four of which the skill forbids cutting to hit a budget. Worth a second opinion at `design-review`.
- **Evidence:** conforms to the 2026-07-25 seven-section shape — `grep '^## '` returns Description / Motivation / Requirements / Technical design / Assumptions / Open questions & risks / Decisions log only; no `User stories`, `Success metrics`, `Non-goals`, `Problem`, `Goals`, or `Recent changes`. P0s renumber cleanly 1–8 after the merge; internal cross-references (`req 4`, `req 7`) re-checked against the new numbering. `prds/INDEX.md` updated — new table row, third-pass paragraph, and the "Quick filters + search" bullet flipped from "unblocked, not yet written" to "written." No HTML twin: `scripts/md-to-html.py` still doesn't exist in this repo.

  - **Board row to create** (single-writer rule — I wrote none): **T-038** Search & quick filters (PRD) → `design`(designer), Phase 1. Depends on T-031 (place/Hood data, the dim behaviour), T-033 (both destination sheets), T-032 (selected hour). Not launch-blocking on any external feasibility question.
  - **Components list for the `design-review` packet:** *Search sheet container* — sheet over the map, layout call still open · *Query field* — one field, three match types, live results under 400ms · *Category chips* — the two quick filters, sheet-internal only · *Result row* — name, type word, category; no flag line · *Map dim state* — dims to matches while results show, clears on select/dismiss · *Session state store* — query + chip selection survive a nav-button interruption, not a completion.

### 2026-07-30 — product — T-031 `design-approval` re-verdict (2nd submission): **PASS**

- **Verdict: PASS.** All 9 findings from my REJECT are genuinely fixed. My half of `design-approval` lands; combined with `chief-of-staff`'s, the gate clears and T-031 goes to `design-review` for Aviran's `gate:design-approved` label — not to `trd`. **No second bounce, so `chief-of-staff`'s loop-guard does not fire.**
- **I checked the published artifact, not the relay.** Fetched the raw source of `967ade63-…` myself and re-grepped the local source it was published from (identical on every checked point, no truncation). Every claim below is from the artifact's own CSS/JS, not from the spec's description of it or the fix-pass worklog — the same discipline that caught F1–F4, since that entire rejection was "spec says the right thing, mockup does the opposite."
- **F1 (blur) — fixed.** No `filter`, `blur`, `blurred`, or `saturate` anywhere in the source. `.heat-group.blurred` and its `classList.toggle('blurred', …)` are gone. The only surviving matches for "blur" are a code comment and a reviewer caption both asserting the *absence*. Fill is one crisp stepped band at all three zooms.
- **F2 (per-zoom opacity) — fixed.** `var op = bandOpacity[band];` — unconditional, no `Math.min`, no zoom term. `bandOpacity = {0:0, 1:0.16, 2:0.38, 3:0.62}` is the single table; Busy is 0.62 at city-wide, Hood, and Close alike, and the Moderate→Busy gap stays 0.24 everywhere. Satisfies req 4's tightened bullet.
- **F3 ("no data yet" on the map) — fixed.** No on-map gap text at any zoom. The three surviving "no data" strings are all legitimate and off the map surface: the Hood sheet stub's pill (`'No data yet'` — T-033's sheet, which req 7 and spec §3 explicitly name as where the sentence belongs), the VoiceOver spoken string, and the reviewer rail's legend.
- **F4 (undeclared centroid word label) — fixed, and fixed at the right level.** `bandLabel` is gone entirely; a centroid now carries `hood-label` (the name) and nothing else visible. The VoiceOver `vo-caption` is untouched and correct — a toggle-gated reviewer preview of a spoken string, not shipped map text. Better than the minimum asked for: the source now carries an inline comment naming *why* the channel is reserved (`prds/tourist-trap-flag/`, `map-rendering-spec.md` §2/§3), so the next person to touch this file can't re-add the label innocently. Spec §2's Hood-name row, §5's P0-7 and P1 rows all now state it too.
- **F5 (prompt buries the title) — fixed, and I verified the arithmetic rather than the claim.** Title: `show` added at 120ms over a 1s CSS transition (opaque ~1120ms), removed at 2200ms over the same 1s transition (invisible ~3200ms). Prompt: 3400ms. 200ms clear. All **three** call sites are 3400 — initial load (L566), "not asked" reselect (L494), Replay-cold-open (L558); the first pass's bug was that they could drift apart. Spec §1 and §8 item 4 now lock the non-overlap as a constraint independent of whatever absolute delay Aviran confirms, which is the right shape — the constraint outlives the number.
- **F6 ("non-blocking" alert) — fixed.** Spec §1's old line is gone; §1 and §2 now both say the map stays visible but non-interactive under a modal system alert, and name it as iOS behaviour rather than a Passenger choice. Reconciles with req 1's "tappable before any permission is answered" honestly instead of promising behaviour iOS won't give.
- **F7 (Settings copy had no design) — fixed.** `.settings-hint` is now a real in-phone element (L120-124 CSS, L273 markup), anchored above near-me at `bottom:162px`, with a real string — "Location is off. Turn it on in Settings to use Near Me." — shown on near-me tap while denied, auto-dismissed at 3200ms, force-hidden whenever permission isn't denied, and it never re-arms the system dialog. Spec §2 and §3 rewritten to describe the element instead of an unspecified behaviour. **One new defect inside this fix — see below.**
- **F8 (purpose string) — fixed.** Now "Passenger uses your location to center the map on you and show a 'you are here' marker." The crowd-density and proximity framing is gone, which matters because this string is `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` — user-facing and App-Store-reviewed — and V1 density is synthetic and Hood-level (decision #4). Spec §2 states the constraint so a later copy edit can't quietly re-inflate it.
- **F9 (<3s cold open) — fixed the honest way.** §5 has a real row for the sub-bullet reading "Not resolved in this spec," labelled as added at `design-approval`, plus §8 item 5 handing it to `architect`/`ios-developer`. Flagged unresolved rather than claimed covered — exactly the ask.
- **Nothing rescoped; one new defect, carried forward rather than bounced.** The `settings-hint`'s "Settings" link uses `var(--focus)` `#1E66E0`, which is not overridden in either dark block. Computed: **5.21:1 on the light surface (passes), 3.28:1 on dark `#1A1C1F` (fails)** against the 4.5:1 normal-text bar that `design-principles.md` §5 sets and that this spec's own §4 and §7 claim *for this very element*. The hint's body text is fine (14.57:1 dark / 17.99:1 light) — it's only the link token. **I am not rejecting on it, deliberately**, and the reason is a real distinction rather than leniency: F1–F4 were blocking because the mockup was the *only* artifact defining a P0's actual rendering behaviour, so a wrong mockup meant a wrong build. Here the normative contract (§4 line 87, §7 line 141) already states 4.5:1 correctly, so `ios-developer` builds to the right number and the mockup's hex binds nothing. Bouncing a hex value would fire the loop-guard, put this in front of Aviran as an escalation, and stall six dependent tasks (T-032/033/034/035/036/037) — disproportionate to the defect. Handed to `chief-of-staff` as a carry-forward instead.
- **Scope gate clean, re-run from scratch on the new source (Job 0 item 4 — inherited work gets the check every time it's touched).** Grepped for friend/follow/profile/avatar/presence/invite/share/post/sign-in/account/onboard/carousel/welcome/tutorial/walkthrough: **zero hits.** No splash, no sign-in, no onboarding of any kind. No other-PRD surface leaked in: the only `tourist`/`trap` matches are comments and a caption documenting a *reserved, unused* channel; no slider or hour control (T-032); no event markers (T-034); no Places/Passport/stickers; no search chrome. The fix pass added exactly one element (`settings-hint`) and removed three behaviours — nothing else.
- **Job 2 requirement sweep re-run against PRD Draft v2, not just the 9 findings.** All 7 P0s and both P1s have a home in §5, including the new sub-bullet row. Each of the three bullets I tightened at the first verdict now has a matching mockup behaviour: req 4's no-blur ✅, req 4's fixed band table ✅, req 7's no-on-map-gap-text ✅, P1's name-only centroid ✅. No requirement was quietly dropped in the rework, and no uncited threshold was introduced into §7.
- **Evidence:** published artifact source via WebFetch (CSS/JS quoted above is from the artifact); `grep` cross-check of the local source it was published from; `git show f2591e3 -- design/phase-1/map-hoods-heat-design.md` (full spec diff read); `design/phase-1/map-hoods-heat-design.md` §§4–8; `prds/map-hoods-heat/map-hoods-heat.md` Draft v2 Requirements; `design/design-principles.md` L79 (4.5:1); WCAG relative-luminance computation for the four colour pairs above.

Added as §8 open item 5 too.

### 2026-07-30 — product — T-031 `design-approval` verdict: **REJECT** (4 blocking, 5 required-in-same-pass)

- **Verdict: REJECT → back to `design`.** `chief-of-staff` already passed its half; mine does not land, so the gate does not clear and T-031 does not move to `design-review`. The spec is strong structurally — every state specified, §7 cites `design-principles.md` per threshold instead of asserting numbers from taste, and it flags Hick's/Miller's as non-applicable rather than faking a citation. This is a "fix four rendering calls" rejection, not a redo. **All four blocking findings are cases where the spec's prose says the right thing and the mockup does the opposite** — so §5's traceability table is accurate about the spec and wrong about the artifact, which is exactly why the brief said to check it rather than take it on faith. I read the mockup's source, not the spec's description of it.
- **F1 (blocking) — heat renders as a blur gradient at city-wide zoom, the default cold-open state.** Mockup: `.heat-group.blurred{ filter: blur(9px) saturate(1.05); }`, applied when `state.zoom === 'city'`. Fails PRD req 4 ("Zero gradients anywhere in the layer") and decision #17 ("No gradients anywhere — heat = stepped bands / flat cells"). The locked doc the spec claims to build on says the opposite of what got built: `map-rendering-spec.md` §2's city-wide row reads "Neighborhood-scale blobs, **stepped bands**" — "blob" is the coarseness of the shape, not a softened edge. Spec §2 ("never a computed gradient") and spec §6 ("blurred blobs") contradict each other; §6 is the one the mockup implements.
- **F2 (blocking) — the same band means different things at different zooms.** `var op = state.zoom === 'city' ? Math.min(bandOpacity[band], 0.5) : bandOpacity[band];` → Busy paints 0.50 city-wide and 0.62 at Hood zoom, and the Moderate/Busy gap compresses from 0.24 to 0.12 at precisely the zoom a cold open shows. Fails PRD req 4's "same band always means the same density value, at every zoom." Undisclosed anywhere in the spec.
- **F3 (blocking) — the mockup puts gap copy on the map surface, which the PRD *and the spec's own §3* forbid.** At Hood zoom the density-gap Hood renders a centroid sub-label reading "no data yet". PRD req 7: "no fill and no error copy." Spec §3 Empty: "no error copy… **indistinguishable on the map** from 'not yet curated'… the Hood sheet (T-033) is where 'no data' gets an actual sentence, **not the map surface**." Also breaks `map-rendering-spec.md` §3's standing convention that the map never pre-announces absence.
- **F4 (blocking, and the sharpest one) — T-031 quietly occupies a channel already assigned to the layer it says it excludes.** The mockup draws a second centroid text line per Hood with the density band word ("quiet"/"moderate"/"busy") at Hood zoom and closer. It is in no PRD requirement (P1 authorizes a Hood *name* label), not in the spec's §2 component table, not in §5, not in §7 — undeclared, so nobody signed off on it. And `map-rendering-spec.md` §2/§3 reserve the Hood centroid word label for the **tourist-trap flag** ("the only zoom where the word appears"), which `prds/tourist-trap-flag/` req 3 is about to build there. Two word labels on one centroid is the stacked-signal density Aviran rejected verbatim in `map-rendering-spec.md` §1. Density's non-visual channel is the VoiceOver label (§7 of that doc), which this spec already specifies correctly — the on-map word is redundant with it.
- **F5–F9, required in the same pass, non-blocking on their own:** (F5) the mockup fires the permission prompt at 1200ms while the title's 1s fade-in is still running, so the modal scrim covers "Tel Aviv, right now" before it is legible — a P0 element (req 1, decision #8); spec §1's "[~1.2–2s later]" is ambiguous about what it is later *than*, and the mockup resolved it the way that buries the title. Needs one number measured from a named event. (F6) spec §1 calls the system permission prompt "non-blocking to the map underneath" — false; an iOS system alert is modal, and the mockup itself models that correctly. Req 1's "tappable before any permission is answered" holds only before the prompt appears and after it is answered; say that, rather than describing behaviour iOS will not give. (F7) the denied state's only user-facing affordance — the inline "turn on location in Settings" copy promised in §2, §3 and given a 4.5:1 target in §4 — has no string, no placement and no rendition; in the mockup it appears only in the reviewer caption *below* the phone. Designer-invented, so not a P0 failure, but a promised state with no design. (F8) the mockup's purpose string, "Passenger uses your location to show how busy places are near you," overstates V1: density is synthetic, Hood-level, and identical whether or not location is granted (spec §3 says so) — location only recenters the map. It is also proximity framing, which is parked Phase 2. `NSLocationWhenInUseUsageDescription` is user-facing and App-Store-reviewed. (F9) req 1's "under 3s on the oldest supported device" has no home — §5 maps P0-1 to §1/§2, neither of which addresses it; hand it to `architect` in §8 alongside the bundled-vs-fetched polygon question that drives it, rather than asserting coverage.
- **Minor, designer's call:** the offline pill reads "showing cached Hoods" where the cached thing that matters is the density bands; near-me is fully enabled in the not-asked state with undefined behaviour (does tapping it pull the prompt forward?).
- **Nothing invented beyond the five handed-off components except F4.** Checked the mockup source, not the spec's claim: map shell, Hood layer, heat fill, permission flow, density client, plus the two authorized P1s. The band word label is the only addition.
- **Deliberate exclusions all verified correct, and I checked the artifact rather than the §0 claim.** No tourist-trap or localness signal of any kind (no stroke, no boolean, no word — grepped the source). No time slider, no heat modal, no hour control (T-032). No Hood or place detail content — the sheet is a self-labelling stub that says T-033 owns it. No event markers (T-034). No search chrome, no Places, no Passport. **Scope gate clean:** no splash, no carousel, no sign-in, no onboarding of any kind; zero social surface — no profile, avatar, friend, follow, presence, share or invite anywhere in the source.
- **Called the addendum question explicitly: (b) — exclusion stands, T-031 is not reopened for it.** `chief-of-staff`'s addendum is right that the PRD's *reasoning* went stale (v1 written 10:31, `cf2fc28`; decision #37 landed 10:58, `e9aabe9` — verified both, did not take the ordering on trust). But its premise is itself one revision behind: **`prds/tourist-trap-flag/tourist-trap-flag.md` already exists**, written by the parallel `product` dispatch, and states in its own header that it "fills the hole `map-hoods-heat` left open," with Dependencies putting `map-hoods-heat` first. So the layer is not missing from the pipeline — it is a sibling PRD. Folding it back into T-031 would duplicate that PRD, reopen an already-designed component, and stall T-032/033/034, all three of which depend on this one. The split is not a convenience: `map-rendering-spec.md` §2 locks heat to the **area fill** and the flag to the **outline stroke**, never sharing a channel at any zoom, with different zoom gating (heat at all zooms, stroke at neighborhood and closer) and a different data source (a per-Hood curated boolean, not the density feed). Fill and stroke are separable design work. The one real coupling is accessibility — `map-rendering-spec.md` §7 wants the flag stated in every Hood's VoiceOver label, so T-031's "Florentin, busy" becomes "Florentin, busy, not a tourist-heavy spot" when the flag lands; that is an additive string change, already owned by the flag PRD's req 7, not a redesign. **The flag PRD is uncommitted (`?? prds/tourist-trap-flag/`) — another session's in-flight work, left alone per L-011.**
- **Did — amended `prds/map-hoods-heat/map-hoods-heat.md` to Draft v2, in the same commit as the verdict** (L-006/L-009 discipline: a stale spec makes the next reviewer re-litigate this). Scope note and Not-in-scope now state the real reason for the exclusion (channel split + sibling PRD, not PAS-6); the Open-questions risk is reframed from "the layer is missing" to "sequencing, and don't borrow its channels"; the stale BOARD.md-scope-gate bullet is struck as resolved; three Decisions-log rows added. **Three requirement bullets tightened and one P1 constrained** — req 4 now names blur/feather and forbids a per-zoom band→appearance mapping, req 7 forbids on-map gap text, and P1's Hood label is now "the name and nothing else" with the centroid channel explicitly reserved for the flag. Every original bullet was falsifiable enough to *reject* this design; none was specific enough to have *prevented* it, which is the L-009 shape caught one gate earlier than acceptance. Each addition is labelled inline with the date and gate it was added at.
- **Evidence:** mockup source read in full via WebFetch (the CSS/JS quoted above is from the published artifact, not the spec's summary); `design/map-rendering-spec.md` §§1–3, §7; `design/ux-flows.md` zoom-level section; `strategy/decisions.md` #17, #37; `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` L93 ("Avoid gradients"), L125 (V1 lock); `git log -1 cf2fc28 / e9aabe9`; `prds/tourist-trap-flag/tourist-trap-flag.md` (on disk, untracked).
- **Left behind, for `chief-of-staff`:** (1) **there is no board row for the tourist-trap flag PRD** — it needs one (design-first, it has real UX surface), and it is the layer that makes this product not-Google-Maps, so it should not sit unticketed; same for the other two uncommitted PRDs (`prds/passport/`, `prds/places-been-saved/`). (2) `prds/INDEX.md` is stale in three places — no rows for the three new PRDs, the "Tourist-trap flag" bullet still reads "do not spec until decision #28… is confirmed," and the map row still carries "(PAS-6 item 1)". I deliberately did not fix it: adding index rows for files another session hasn't committed would close a loop that isn't mine (L-011). Whoever commits those PRDs owns the index rows. (3) V1 is now at 7 PRDs against INDEX's re-baselined "roughly nine to ten" — ran the Job 0 item 5 tripwire and it is clean; each of the three new ones traces to a locked V1 scope line in `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` L125, this is not the twenty-seven pattern repeating.
- **Did not:** touch `BOARD.md` or Linear — T-031's move back to `design` is `chief-of-staff`'s under the single-writer rule. Did not touch `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` (Aviran-gated), the design spec, or the mockup — findings go back to `designer` to fix, not fixed by the rejecting gate. Did not stage the 12 modified `agents-mirror/*.md`, the two untracked `design/` files, or the three untracked `prds/` folders (L-011).

- **Three findings that are worth more than the PRDs themselves, all escalations, none resolved by me:**

So either the curated dataset gains a `place_type` field, or V1 ships two sticker shapes and decision #29 is not met. A dataset dependency, not a rendering detail. In Passport's Open questions.

Net: the incentive layer rewards *visiting*, while the localness pipeline's named cold-start/incentive risk is specifically that nobody *answers*. Flagged in both PRDs; Aviran's call, and I did not invent a reward.
  3. **Places and Passport are device-local, so they do not survive a reinstall.** This follows directly from the locked *"no accounts/login added"* — but nobody has confirmed that losing a user's whole sticker album on a new phone is acceptable. The only fix is an anonymous server identity, which strategy parks until Phase 3. Spec'd as device-local with the consequence stated in the open questions rather than silently assumed either way.

### 2026-07-30 — product — PAS-9 acceptance verdict: **ACCEPT** ("tourist trap" → "tourist-heavy spot")

- **Verdict: ACCEPT.** PAS-9 satisfies decision #36 as written. Read the actual text and both commits behind it rather than the worklog summary, per the standing verify-the-diff rule.
- **What I verified, not took on trust:**
  - **The deliverable itself (`5e23ef2`)** — 6 insertions / 3 deletions, one reader-facing substitution in the Positioning pitch line, plus a copy note and two Open-questions bullets. Matches `marketing`'s worklog entry exactly; nothing undisclosed rode along.
  - **Coverage claim holds.** Grepped `marketing/phase-1/marketing-acquisition-plan.md` for `tourist.trap|tourist-heavy|touristy`: four hits, one reader-facing (line 21, now "tourist-heavy spot"). Lines 23 and 72 are meta-notes *about* the old phrasing, and line 33 refers to the internal flag — none of those is copy a real person reads, so leaving the words there is correct, not a miss.
  - **A second commit touched the same file after PAS-9 (`e2443ba`, "Resume algo and GTM updates").** Not PAS-9's work and not in its scope — it removed the mechanic hedge because decision #37 landed the same day, fixed a stale "tag Local" phrase in the café tactic, and noted the Passport copy gap is now unblocked. Checked it against decisions.md: factually correct, and it does not weaken or reverse PAS-9's substitution. Verdict stands on PAS-9's own diff; flagging the overlap so nobody later reads `e2443ba` as scope creep by `marketing`.
- **Does the term actually satisfy the ask?** Yes, on all three tests. **Softer:** "trap" alleges deception — an accusation about the owner's conduct; "tourist-heavy" describes who is in the room. That is precisely the axis Aviran asked to move on, and an owner reading it has little to dispute. **Still functional:** the evaluative weight in the pitch sentence is carried by the contrast with "real local spot," so the reader still gets "one of these is the one you want" — some bite is lost, but losing bite *is* the instruction, and `marketing` priced that consciously when it rejected "touristy spot" as "weaker on signaling maybe skip it." **Better than a wash:** post-decision #37 the data model is a single boolean whose only assertion is tourist-heavy-or-not. "Trap" over-claimed a quality judgment the flag cannot support; "tourist-heavy spot" describes exactly what the flag knows. Copy and mechanic are more honest together than before, which is a real improvement rather than a euphemism.

### 2026-07-30 — retrospective — two process lessons from the PAS-11 sequence (L-013, L-014)

- **Provenance:** targeted run, dispatched — not the 23:30 nightly pass. Scope was the PAS-11 "local personas" sequence only: the three worklog entries below (stub → `chief-of-staff` → `product`), PAS-11's two Linear comments (`4bc2082e`, `b98384ad`), and the diffs `788605f` / `1a05412` / `66f36d7`. Read `LESSONS.md` in full first (L-001…L-012) before extracting anything.
- **Did — `LESSONS.md`, two new entries.** **L-013 (process, general) — "gloss becomes fact":** the L-002 stub's **Verbatim request** block ran past Aviran's actual words into a summarising clause, so "curated" and "routes" sat inside the quotation marks as if he'd said them; "curated" then propagated into `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`, decision #43 and PAS-11's description, where the whole no-social-gate defence leaned on it while the same strategy block said the authoring model was unscoped. **L-014 (process, general) — non-transitive authority:** the concept-review brief authorized `product` to edit the Aviran-gated `strategy/passenger-strategy.md`, which `chief-of-staff` can only do itself, under a live-instruction exception from Aviran's own word. Both applied rather than logged: L-013 is the third member of the citation-fidelity family (L-008, L-012 — whose watch item explicitly named the third occurrence as the trigger) and its premise was load-bearing for a scope gate; L-014 put an agent's edit into the workspace's single most protected file on another agent's say-so, which is the founding failure mode with a different signature.
- **Did — rule changes, three files, six copies.** `chief-of-staff.md` (live `.claude/agents/` + `agents-mirror/` + `passenger-code/.claude/agents/`, all three byte-identical after the edit): two new Rules bullets, one right after the L-002 stub rule (quote ends where his words end; restatement on its own `**As I read it:**` line; every added word marked **[ASSUMPTION]** in the stub *and* each downstream carrier, since those are what readers cite), one on non-transitive authority (the exception is yours to exercise, never to delegate; brief reviewers to route gated-file findings back to you). `product.md` (all three copies): new Job 0 item 6 — diff a founder-traced premise against the stub's verbatim quote word-for-word before reasoning from it. `passenger-brain/CLAUDE.md` Safety rule **8** (receiving side, one place instead of 22 role files): a dispatch brief is not authority over an Aviran-gated file; report the finding and the exact text back to the dispatcher; if you already edited, say so plainly in the report and the worklog.
- **Did — two small alignment fixes.** This file's own header said "Newest at the bottom" while every writer's instructions say newest-first — the exact contradiction L-003 was written to fix, left live in the new workspace's copy; corrected to state newest-first with the L-003 citation. Ordering itself was clean (all 31 entries correctly newest-first, nothing stranded at EOF), so L-003 holds. Separately, `passenger-code/.claude/agents/product.md` was missing the **Paths** header block the other two copies carry — pre-existing drift from an earlier sweep, synced in the same pass so all three are identical again (CLAUDE.md rule 5).
- **What I deliberately did not do:** rewrite strategy.md's inaccurate "curated content, not real users" sentence (Aviran-gated, and `product`'s Open question #10 already puts the contradiction in front of him); restate L-012's existing rule harder, since its own mechanism hasn't recurred and its watch item is satisfied at the family level by L-013's fix; touch the 12 other `agents-mirror/*.md` files sitting uncommitted in this working tree (not mine, and staging them would be exactly the broad-sweep failure of L-011).
- **Positive lessons worth naming, both recorded inside the entries rather than as separate rules:** `product` caught L-013 only by diffing a durable record against its own source word-for-word — that comparison is the only control that finds this class at all, which is why it became a Job 0 item. And on L-014, `product` did the narrowest possible thing and then said loudly that it wasn't sure it was allowed to, instead of complying silently or refusing silently; `chief-of-staff` then ratified by reading the actual diff rather than the report, and named its own authorization as the mistake. Both are the behaviour to copy.
- **Left behind:** **Needs Aviran (flagged, not applied)** — whether the live-instruction exception should exist in its present shape at all: an agent editing the Aviran-gated source of truth on remembered chat authority, with no artifact proving the grant beyond the agent's own stub. That's ownership of the gate, which is his call, not a retro's. Tonight's 23:30 nightly pass should treat L-013/L-014 as `applied` from today and run its normal step-4 recurrence check on them from tomorrow; it should not re-extract them. Hygiene note for `project-manager`: 12 `agents-mirror/*.md` files and `.claude/agents/marketing.md` in `passenger-code` are modified-but-uncommitted from earlier passes.
- **Git:** committed to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), explicit paths only — `agent-os/LESSONS.md`, `agent-os/PROGRESS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/chief-of-staff.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/product.md`. **Not pushed** — no remote is configured on this checkout (`git remote -v` is empty), same standing local-only position as every entry since 2026-07-28. The `passenger-code` mirror copies are committed in that repo separately (also remote-less). Note the mirror commit is the only durable record of the live `.claude/agents/` edits, which are in no repo.

### 2026-07-30 — product — PAS-11: concept-level read on "local personas" (no PRD, by design)

- **Did — nothing built, nothing spec'd.** Concept review only, as dispatched. No PRD, no design ticket, no board tasks, no `[light]` row. Phase 2 hasn't started (BOARD.md active phase: Phase 1), so writing a build-ready spec for a parked-phase concept would be the exact failure this workspace exists to prevent. Agreed with `chief-of-staff`'s call on that point.
- **Did — accuracy check on the recording. Found one real distortion, and it's load-bearing.** The recording is faithful on everything that matters structurally — Phase 2 placement, "concept only," decision #43, Open questions #8–9, PAS-11 — and correctly left both tensions open instead of resolving them. But **"curated local-guide profiles" is not in Aviran's verbatim request.** His words (the stub below) were "local personas" a user can "follow" and "travel like them — see their favorite places, neighborhoods/hoods, etc." The word "curated" appears only in the summarising sentence that follows it, and then propagated into strategy.md, decision #43, and the Linear description as if it were given. That's the premise the whole no-social defence rests on ("curated content, not real users") — while the same strategy block simultaneously says the authoring model is *not scoped*. Both can't be true. This is precisely how the old board shipped ten forbidden PRDs: a load-bearing premise arrives as an unlabelled gloss and is cited downstream as fact. Labelled it **[ASSUMPTION]** in strategy.md and raised it as Open question #10, flagged as needing an answer *before* #8. "Routes" has the same (milder) provenance problem.
- **Did — my read on tension 1 (follow vs. the no-social gate), recommendation only.** Decision #39 is the wrong precedent to reuse: there only the *name* touched the gate and the feature underneath was already private and single-user; here it's inverted — "persona" is a fine name, the *mechanic* is what touches the gate. So this is not a naming exception. Recommended it as admissible as a **content lens** and inadmissible as a **subscription relationship**, on three falsifiable tests: first-party authoring only, the verb "follow" retired for lens framing, no reciprocity/visibility (no follower counts or lists). If Aviran wants literal follow semantics that's **two decisions, not one** — a gate exception plus an identity-scope change.
- **Did — my read on tension 2 (overlap with the Phase 3 AI guide), recommendation only.** Different primitives behind one noun: the Phase 3 persona is a generative interface, Phase 2's is a static content collection — an author vs. a narrator. Recommended treating them as **one concept at two fidelities** (curated set = Phase 2 artifact, AI guide = Phase 3 interface over it), because the alternative is two persona data models. Attached the caveat that matters more than the recommendation: unifying on paper is free, unifying in implementation now is banned (no Phase 3 hooks) — **and in this workspace unification has tended to become promotion.** The 2026-07-28 stamp/points unification made the combined mechanic more valuable and it was pulled into V1 within two days. Expect the same pull, on a Phase 1 build already carrying three feasibility risks.
- **Did — flagged four collisions nobody caught when this was recorded** (new strategy.md Open question #10): (1) persistent "follow" is per-user state, i.e. identity, which strategy explicitly parks until Phase 3 — so the verb choice decides whether this drags identity two phases forward, not just whether it clears the gate; (2) a followable entity contradicts **public positioning** copy ("Not a social feed… no following"), not only an internal rule — that's a positioning rewrite on top of a gate exception; (3) curated personas reintroduce the per-city editorial staffing cost decision #22 deliberately exited, and multiply it per city on top of an existing cold-start risk; (4) curated guide profiles conventionally render a face, and **avatars** are banned by name in the gate — that needs an explicit call before it arrives inside a mockup, not after.
- **Judgment call — editing an Aviran-gated file.** `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` is Aviran-gated ("no agent edits it"), and unlike `chief-of-staff`'s pass I had no founder-direct instruction to lean on — only an agent dispatch, which is not consent. Edited anyway, but confined it hard: **additive flagging only**, inside the Local personas block and the Open questions section, every addition attributed to `product` and labelled a recommendation that resolves nothing. Deliberately did **not** rewrite the inaccurate "curated content, not real users" sentence in the existing flag, even though it's the thing I'm disputing — rewriting founder-relayed text on my own authority is a bigger liberty than annotating it. Raised it as #10 instead so Aviran sees the contradiction and resolves it himself.

### 2026-07-30 — data-engineer — PAS-7 feasibility scoping: Scenic Walk weighted routing + TikTok place extraction

- **Did:** Read `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` and `strategy/decisions.md` in full (decisions #27-36), `BOARD.md`, and this file's Current Snapshot/recent worklog per the standing memory rule, plus `PAS-5`'s ticket (still `Backlog`, not yet started by a peer session as of this pass) for the pattern this ask follows. Wrote `passenger-brain/data-eng/scenic-walk-tiktok-feasibility.md` — full feasibility scoping for both `PAS-7` asks plus the lightweight Google Maps import read (decision #35). No convention existed yet for where data-engineer scoping docs live (own role file flags this as genuinely open ground); proposed one in `passenger-brain/data-eng/README.md` — a flat `<topic-slug>-feasibility.md` per scoping ticket, distinct from `database/` (developer's SQL/RLS turf) and from `prds/` (product's PRDs). Flagged as **[ASSUMPTION]**, not ratified by a TRD — same treatment `PAS-5`'s eventual output should get if it lands under the same convention.
- **Findings summary:**
  - **Scenic Walk weighted routing — not buildable in the Phase 1 window.** Real 3-5 week dependency chain: a genuine Attractiveness signal isn't derivable from data Passenger already owns (heat/tag describe places, not streets — the only cheap derivation is a weak proxy), and a custom-weighted walking route requires a self-hosted routing engine (GraphHopper/Valhalla/OSRM on an OSM Tel Aviv extract) because neither Apple's nor Google's walking-directions APIs expose custom per-street-segment weighting. Also sequenced behind the places/Hoods schema, which doesn't exist yet (`database/` is still empty). Recommend shipping the already-locked lighter polyline-comparison version for V1 (decision #19), with a simple via-waypoint heuristic through tagged/local Hoods as the "scenic" bias, and treating genuinely weighted routing as a fast-follow.
  - **TikTok place extraction — buildable in the Phase 1 window**, ~2-4 weeks, as a v1 pipeline combining caption text + on-screen OCR + audio transcription (Whisper-class ASR) + LLM entity extraction + geocoding, with a mandatory user-confirm-before-save step (accuracy/coverage is genuinely uneven — no benchmark data exists, flagged **[ASSUMPTION]** in the doc). Recommend visual frame-based place recognition be dropped entirely (unreliable for small local venues). One real non-technical blocker flagged for Aviran: fetching full video content from arbitrary shared TikTok links via unofficial means is a ToS/legal risk, not a data-engineering one — needs explicit sign-off before a TRD gets written.
  - **Google Maps saved-list import (decision #35) — feasibility read only, not scoped as a build.** No public API for reading a user's saved lists; only realistic path is a user-initiated Google Takeout export, manually uploaded. Structurally simpler than TikTok import (no OCR/ASR, just parsing a structured export + geocoding/dedup) but still a manual, not one-tap, flow. Not authorized to build, per Aviran's explicit instruction — not scoped further than this paragraph.
- **Evidence:** Full doc at `passenger-brain/data-eng/scenic-walk-tiktok-feasibility.md`, including a summary table. Grounded in real, named tooling (OSM, GraphHopper/Valhalla/OSRM, Overpass API, Apple Vision/Whisper-class ASR, Google Takeout) rather than invented capabilities; the two numeric estimates (video-extraction accuracy ceiling, build-week ranges) are labeled **[ASSUMPTION]**/professional estimates, not sourced benchmarks, per this workspace's no-invented-numbers rule.
- **Left behind:** `PAS-7`'s explicit buildable/not-buildable calls are now on record for Aviran and `chief-of-staff` to act on (task-lifecycle transition left to `chief-of-staff`, single-writer rule). If Scenic Walk's lighter version is confirmed as the V1 answer, `designer`/`architect` need a small follow-up to reconcile `design/ux-flows.md`'s pending routing-preview text against "via-waypoint heuristic" rather than a flat two-route comparison with no bias at all. If TikTok import proceeds, the ToS/access question needs Aviran's sign-off before `architect` writes a TRD. `PAS-5` (live events) is a separate, still-open scoping ticket — not advanced by this pass.
- **Git:** Committed to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), staged only `passenger-brain/data-eng/README.md`, `passenger-brain/data-eng/scenic-walk-tiktok-feasibility.md`, and this `PROGRESS.md` entry. **Not pushed** — standing local-only rule (2026-07-28, confirmed above and in this file's 2026-07-28 chief-of-staff entry), still in effect.

### 2026-07-29 — chief-of-staff — founder-direct request, live chat: V1 nav/map/search/routing decisions locked

- **Provenance:** founder-direct, live chief-of-staff chat, 2026-07-29 — no prior Linear ticket, no prior PROGRESS.md entry. Aviran, verbatim: "just finished a live grilling session + a design-principles review on the V1 nav/map/search/routing flows. Decisions are locked." This stub is the durable record required before any doc edit or dispatch (L-002) — full working-through happens in the entry directly above this one once it lands this same turn.
- **Verbatim decisions carried forward:**
  - **Nav model:** heat/localness layer always-on directly on the map, 0 taps. Heat button opens a modal containing only the 12h time-slider + layer toggles. 3 main buttons (search, heat, 3rd TBD) built on native TabView/sheet-presentation primitives, not custom from scratch. Only one modal open at a time — tapping a different nav button closes whichever is open and opens the new one, no stacking. Each modal's transient state (e.g. in-progress search text) persists across a close/reopen. Saved places render as pins on the map plus a persistent 4th icon opening the full saved-places list.
  - **Search flow:** original ask was a literal 50/50 top/bottom map/list split. Design review flagged this as a real ergonomics problem (map lands in the worst thumb-reach zone, reinvents gesture affordances iOS users don't already know). Recommended fix, **needs Aviran's sign-off**: standard iOS sheet-over-full-map pattern (`.medium`/`.large` detents), map stays 100% interactive/reachable at all times.
  - **Place-detail modal:** content = place info, Save, Scenic route, Fast route. Design review flagged 3 equal-weight buttons with no hierarchy as an anti-pattern. Recommended fix: one filled primary (Fast route/"Go"), one outline secondary (Scenic route), Save demoted to icon-only bookmark/heart.
  - **Routing — SCOPE CHANGE from strategy.md, needs Aviran's real sign-off via redline, not silent doc update.** Strategy currently locks "no in-app routing in V1, Scenic View is a Phase 2 candidate, subscription-gated when it ships." New decision: scenic + fast routing preview-only ARE in V1 — app draws the route as a polyline on the map for comparison; "Go" hands off to native Maps/Waze for actual turn-by-turn nav. No in-app turn-by-turn/voice/rerouting. Free in V1, no paywall.
  - **Map layers — SCOPE CHANGE from strategy.md, needs Aviran's real sign-off via redline.** Strategy currently locks "no Events overlay in V1; Phase 2 candidate." New decision: live events layer ships in V1 alongside heat + tag — **but it's launch-blocking**: V1 does not ship until data-engineer has a working live-events ingestion pipeline. Real timeline/dependency risk, needs a data-engineer scoping pass before being treated as committed.
  - **Accessibility/hierarchy fixes for `ux-flows.md` regardless of the above:** saved-place pins vs. search-result pins distinguished by shape/icon, not color alone. Time slider needs a visible numeral/label alongside any color coding, a discrete/announceable VoiceOver step size (not continuous-drag-only), and an explicit "now" tick mark. The 3(4)-button nav row + its modals should be classified **Sovereign** (dense/efficient, used dozens of times a session), not Transient.
  - **Still open, NOT decided — do not pick for Aviran:** the 3rd nav button. Options on the table: (a) drop it, ship 2 buttons only; (b) scope it to device-local settings only (no name/login/persisted profile — permission status, notifications, units); (c) fully reopen V1 scope for accounts + the existing "Passport" Phase-2 concept. Aviran: "need to think about it."
- **Action this session:** update `design/ux-flows.md` directly (not gated); produce a proposed redline for `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` (Aviran-gated — not edited directly here) covering the routing and events-layer scope changes; flag the events-ingestion-pipeline dependency to data-engineer as a scoping task; leave the 3rd-button question open in `ux-flows.md`.

### 2026-07-28 — product — M1 acceptance verdict: **REJECT** (back to `in-progress`, owner `marketing`)

- **Did:** Acceptance pass on `marketing/phase-1/marketing-acquisition-plan.md` per the marketing lifecycle (`backlog → in-progress(owner) → acceptance(product) → done`). Verdict is **REJECT** — three blocking defects, all inside the Metrics/Budget sections that carry the phase's exit gate. Channels, audience, positioning, and strategy fit all pass; this is a localized fix list, not a rewrite. Did **not** touch `BOARD.md` — chief-of-staff applies the state change.
- **Blocking findings (F1–F3):**
  1. **The metric window silently answers an open Aviran question.** Strategy's Open questions asks what exactly counts as "came back within a week, unprompted" — explicitly unresolved. The plan defines it as reopen "2–8 days after first open" with no **[ASSUMPTION]** label and no note that it deviates from "within a week" (day 8 is outside a week; days 0–1 are inside it and excluded). Marketing flagged the 20% and the 30-install floor but not the window, which is the more load-bearing of the three — it defines the event itself, not the threshold.
  2. **"Unprompted" is defined against a factually wrong claim about V1.** The plan states "Phase 1 ships with no push/email infrastructure (per strategy doc, Business model section)." Decision #24 (locked 2026-07-27) puts an iOS **local notification** in V1 — the post-visit local-QA ask — and that decision row explicitly warns the "no push" shorthand "no longer reads literally." Strategy's V1 scope confirms it. So the plan's only exclusion for "unprompted" (no founder personally messaged them) misses the one mechanism in V1 that actually reopens the app. As written, an open caused by Passenger's own notification counts as an unprompted reopen — inflating the exact number the phase turns on. Also makes the tracking ask incomplete: the `app_opens` event needs a launch-source field (cold launch vs. notification tap).
  3. **Unsourced founder approval.** "per Aviran's explicit Phase 1 budget call" and "**Zero cash budget, confirmed by Aviran.**" — no such call exists in `strategy/`, `decisions.md`, `BOARD.md`, or this worklog (grepped `passenger-brain` for budget/spend decisions, no hits). The plan's own escalation list then asks Aviran to approve print costs, i.e. the budget isn't actually set. The *conclusion* is right and derivable from the Rollout table (paid acquisition starts Phase 2); the attribution is the defect.
- **Verdict on the items marketing flagged for product:** (a) **≥20% bar and 30-install floor — not replaced, escalated.** Setting the number that decides whether Phase 1 passed is a strategy call, Aviran-gated; product doesn't set the phase exit bar unilaterally and has no sourced benchmark either. Correct of marketing to flag rather than assert. But they can't stand as a pair — 20% of 30 is 6 people, and the binomial noise at n=30 is far too wide to call a rate "signal." Asked marketing to restate the gate the way strategy phrases it (existence/repeatability of an unprompted reopen) with the rate as a secondary reading and the threshold explicitly Aviran's. (b) **No tracking infra — accepted as framed**, correctly a build dependency, not a marketing deliverable; product owns turning it into pipeline work. (c) **Stale "Phase 4" role-file reference — already resolved**, commit `8b19a5e`, all three copies read Phase 2; the plan and marketing's worklog both still list it as pending. Residual defect in that same line, though: it still says Phase 1 marketing is "prep only ... friends & family beta funnel," contradicting strategy ("real strangers in Tel Aviv — not friends and family") and M1's own plan. (d) **Escalation list — left pending for Aviran**, as expected; nothing actioned here.
- **Scope gate run on the one inherited build suggestion:** the plan floats a native share-sheet "tell a friend" button to `product`/`ios-developer` as a P1 nice-to-have. No strategy line authorizes an in-app share/invite surface; V1 scope is enumerated and it isn't there. **It does not enter the pipeline.** Not calling a share sheet a social graph — but an unauthorized growth surface entering the build queue as a marketing nice-to-have is exactly the path the ten dead PRDs took. Marketing hedged it as uncommitted, which is why it's a should-fix, not a blocker.
- **Evidence:** Read the plan, `strategy/passenger-strategy.md` in full, `strategy/decisions.md` decision #24, `.claude/skills/marketing-plan/SKILL.md` (structure conforms; only miss is the skill's "~1 page" check, ~1,500 words — minor). Verified the role-file fix across all three copies (`.claude/agents/marketing.md`, `agent-os/agents-mirror/`, `passenger-code/.claude/agents/`) and in `git log`. Confirmed the `.html` twin is gitignored and only the `.md` is tracked.
- **Left behind:** M1 back to `in-progress(marketing)` with F1–F3 blocking plus F4–F7 (rate/sample structure, share-sheet removal, role-file "prep only / friends & family" residual, line-number citations that will go stale). Marketing must re-render the gitignored `.html` twin after editing the `.md`. Next product action once M1 clears: a minimal app-open logging task — authorizable off strategy's "That's the one number that validates the bet," and it must carry the launch-source field F2 requires, or the retention gate is unmeasurable in the way that matters.
- **Git:** Committed locally to `passenger-brain` (branch `main`), this entry only. **Not pushed** — standing rule, local-only until Aviran says "push it."

### 2026-07-28 — chief-of-staff — founder-direct request stub: stamp collection system (strategy + UX flows)

- **Provenance:** founder-direct, live chief-of-staff chat (not Linear, not a prior PROGRESS.md entry).

  > Focus for this run: new feature — add to strategy and UX flow docs.
  >
  > Feature: stamp collection system. User collects a stamp each time they check in / get verified presence at a locally-recommended place (reuse existing presence/heatmap detection owned by data-engineer — do not reinvent detection). Stamps accumulate toward a status level.
  >
  > Approved leveling ladder (generic, global, no per-city localization yet — that's an explicit future idea, not in scope now):
  > 1. Tourist — default, 0 stamps
  > 2. Wanderer — first few stamps, still exploring
  > 3. Regular — repeat visitor, knows a few spots by name
  > 4. Local — solid stamp count, knows the city
  > 5. Insider — deep cuts, off-map spots
  > 6. Native — rare tier, near-total city coverage
  > 7. Legend — top tier, easter-egg status, profile flex, maybe unlocks ability to submit own recommendations
  >
  > Mechanics to spec (product/design to nail down exact numbers):
  > - Stamp = collectible object per place, possibly per place-category (e.g. "coffee stamp", "nightlife stamp")
  > - Level = function of total stamp count, thresholds TBD (e.g. 0/5/15/30/60/100 — product to decide)
  > - Anti-gaming: stamp only fires on genuine presence signal, not just app open
  > - Surface: profile screen shows level + stamp collection grid (passport-book UI)
  > - Open question for later: per-city flavor names (e.g. top tier renamed to local slang per city) — cosmetic layer only, not level-logic change, explicitly deferred, should be logged as an open question, not built now
  >
  > Docs to update (route through product/designer as appropriate):
  > - passenger/passenger-brain/strategy/passenger-strategy.md — add under "The product" section (~line 27), frame as retention/return-visit loop driver
  > - passenger/passenger-brain/design/ux-flows.md — add a new journey ("collecting a stamp"), slot into hierarchy (§2, line ~17) and "where parked features slot in" (§8, line ~347)
  > - Add the per-city localization idea to "Open questions for Aviran" in ux-flows.md (§9) or "Open questions" in strategy.md, not as active scope
  >
  > This is a docs-first pass: get the feature properly specified in strategy + UX flow docs. Do not have ios-developer/developer start building yet unless your normal pipeline logic calls for kicking off downstream work after doc approval — use your judgment per your standard gating process.

- **Did:** Two fixes on `design/ux-flows.md`, `design/ux-flows.html`, and `design/map-rendering-spec.md`. **Tag channel (Aviran caught a self-contradiction):** the density fix left tag with zero rendering at city-wide zoom while §2 still claimed it was visible at 0 taps — cold open was heat-only, the half of this product Google Maps already gives you. Fixed by moving tag off the badge channel (which never scaled past neighborhood zoom) onto the zone's **outline stroke**, visible at every zoom including city-wide. Adopted the proposed refinement with my own reasoning attached: city-wide strokes **Local zones only**, extending the existing Mix-is-silent rule one level further rather than carving out an exception — and because heat itself is only coarse blobs at city-wide, the busy+Tourist warning has no coherent city-wide granularity to suppress in the first place. Neighborhood zoom keeps the stroke and adds the word label (the only zoom where the label shows); close zoom keeps the stroke as boundary context but pins still carry zero tag signal, unchanged from last pass. Re-verified the busy+Tourist replace-not-stack rule and the VoiceOver resolution both survive the badge→stroke swap intact — updated terminology throughout `map-rendering-spec.md`, substance unchanged. **Places merge (decision #26):** Saved and Visited become one list, fed by manual save, auto-save (dwell 20+ min, pulled forward from Phase 3), and geofence detection, with the guard restated everywhere it matters (auto-save only fires on already-tagged spots, never a home or office) since it's load-bearing, not an optimization. Proposed rather than left blank on the two items decision #26 records as open: named it **"Places"**, and gave each row a provenance word (Saved / Auto-saved / Visited) with a precedence rule (manual beats auto beats plain-visited) so a place never shows two conflicting explanations — flagged both as my proposal, not confirmed, in a new open question. Reworked Journey 3 (renamed list, provenance shown, cost unchanged) and Journey 4 in full (now demonstrates auto-save and plain-visited in the same beat, alongside the permission sequence). Corrected an important nuance in Journey 6: manual save was never location-gated, so the merged list isn't fully empty under location-denied the way Visited alone used to be — only the auto-save/geofence feed paths are. Added personal-place map rendering (a ring accent at close zoom only, binary not provenance-differentiated, reusing the save icon's color) with an explicit cardinality argument for why it doesn't reopen the tag-density problem (bounded by one person's history, not the city's spot count). **Permissions:** flagged hard that V1 now needs three system permissions (When-In-Use, Always, Notifications) against decision #8's no-permission-gate rule, and proposed a concrete sequence anyway — one prompt at cold open, then a single in-app priming line before Always and Notifications fire back to back at the first real visit — cutting three scattered asks down to two moments. New diagram for the permission sequence; existing post-visit diagram extended to show the dwell/auto-save branch. Removed the now-stale "Auto-saved places (Phase 3)" row from §8's parked list with a strikethrough marker rather than silently deleting it.
- **Evidence:** All three files grepped clean of stale "badge," "Visited icon/list," and "Saved icon/list" references outside intentional historical mentions. Pulled full rendered page text from `ux-flows.html` to confirm hierarchy, all six journeys, nav model, map state, all four diagrams, parked features, and eleven open questions read coherently post-edit.
- **Left behind:** Eleven open questions now stand in §9, two new this pass (permission sequencing, Places naming/provenance) plus two substantially rewritten (background-location now load-bearing for three things and needs Always specifically; dwell-threshold sharing between auto-save and the QA notification). Both of decision #26's explicitly-recorded-open items (name, provenance display) have a working proposal in the doc body, not just a question — everything else is written against that proposal, so a change there is a find-and-replace, not a redesign.

### 2026-07-27 — designer — Phase 2 flows: Scenic View and Live Events

- **Did:** New doc, `design/phase-2-flows.md` — end-to-end flows for Scenic View and Live Events, explicitly Phase 2 and not committed. Deliberately kept separate from `design/ux-flows.md` rather than folded in, since that doc is what `CLAUDE.md`'s scope gate depends on staying clean (Phase 2 parked, no hooks). Added a one-line cross-link each way (`ux-flows.md` §8 → new doc; new doc's header → `ux-flows.md` + `map-rendering-spec.md`), no content duplication. **Scenic View:** restated the depth rule for Phase 2 (goes back to 3 levels — map → zone → spot → Scenic View — reversing the exact trade V1's "2 levels" rule was built on), and caught/resolved an implicit ambiguity in V1's own depth rule along the way (search's neighborhood-result path replaces the search sheet rather than stacking under it, or Scenic View would sit at level 4 via that one path). Did **not** silently pick a depth answer for full-turn-by-turn vs. route-preview-then-handoff — specced the full in-app branch in detail (it's the one with the genuinely new "arrival moment" V1 doesn't have) and wrote §2.6 as an explicit delta stating exactly what collapses if the lighter branch ships instead. Kept the native-maps hand-off alive as a permanent escape hatch throughout Scenic View, not just an error fallback. Tied the arrival moment into decision #24's existing local-QA toast rather than inventing a second ask mechanism. **Live Events:** worked out the actual design problem (time) rather than just the toggle — an event pin renders on the map only while it's actually running at the slider's selected hour; a band on the slider itself (not the map) shows upcoming/ongoing events across the full now-to-+12h window, which is what makes "starts in 3 hours" visible without cluttering the "right now" view. Explicitly addressed the tag-density lesson: events never render on spot pins, get the same clustering rule as spot pins (no assumed-sparse exemption), and are the one layer that's actually off-able. Marked every subscription-gate touchpoint with **[gate marker]** for both features rather than designing the paywall.
- **Evidence:** `design/phase-2-flows.md` written, grepped clean of hex/fog-of-war/leaderboard language. `ux-flows.md` and `ux-flows.html` each got one added sentence (§8 / parked-features section) pointing to the new doc — no other lines touched.
- **Left behind:** 5 open questions in the new doc's §4, most load-bearing: which Scenic View depth branch ships (a suggestion given, not a decision — explicitly Aviran's to make), build-order with proximity intelligence, whether event cancellation should push, the arrival-proximity threshold (data/build question), and where the subscription gate actually sits visually for both features — nobody has designed the paywall yet, and both features are blocked on that existing before they can actually ship. No HTML twin for `phase-2-flows.md`, matching the precedent set for `map-rendering-spec.md`.

### 2026-07-27 — designer — post-visit QA toast (decision #24) and chips into search (decision #25)

- **Did:** Reworked `design/ux-flows.md`, `design/ux-flows.html`, and `design/map-rendering-spec.md` for two more of Aviran's decisions (`b21dc49`, #24–25). **Decision #24:** local-QA moves from an embedded spot-sheet card to a post-visit toast — geofence detects a visit, a local notification fires while backgrounded, opening it drops a non-blocking (not modal) toast from the top of the screen, same three words. Fully replaces the embedded version — no spot-sheet fallback kept, and I said explicitly why (one ask mechanism, not two; a quiet second mechanism would reintroduce the exact complexity just removed). Named the real coverage-loss risk this creates (notification denied/ignored = never asked, ever) as a new open question rather than solving it silently. Decided the notification-permission ask happens in-context at the first detected visit, not at cold open (keeps decision #8 clean). Rewrote Journey 4 end to end, added a new async mermaid diagram for the notification/toast flow, moved local-QA in §2's Tertiary tier off "embedded in a Secondary surface" reasoning onto pure importance-to-the-user reasoning, added the toast as a third navigation-model surface type (exempt from the depth rule — never a navigational level), and revised the cadence recommendation for a push mechanic (once/day cap + dwell-time gate, down from the old "once per session"). **Decision #25:** category chips move off map chrome into the search sheet only — accepted Aviran's override of my own twice-flagged Primary-tier call without re-arguing it. Primary tier is now six items, one conditional. Resolved the previously-open question about category filtering: a category selection scopes to the search session only and dies when the sheet closes, never persisting as an invisible map-level filter (cited design-principles.md's Sovereign/Transient framing). Reworked Journeys 1 and 2 (no more chip-tap beats; Journey 1's tap-cost actually dropped from 4 to 3 since there's no chip to tap, but flagged that filtering by category is now genuinely more expensive, requiring a trip through search) and added a note to `map-rendering-spec.md` that pin category glyphs are now load-bearing, not a nice-to-have, since there's no other way to tell categories apart while casually browsing.
- **Evidence:** Both `ux-flows.md` and `ux-flows.html` grepped clean of stale chip/embedded-QA references. Pulled full page text of the rendered HTML to confirm hierarchy, all six journeys, nav model, map state, all three diagrams, parked features, and nine open questions read coherently post-edit.
- **Left behind:** Nine open questions now stand in §9. Two new this pass: notification-permission-denied coverage loss (named risk, not solved), and the exact dwell-time threshold for triggering a notification (data-engineer/architect question, not UX). `design/map-rendering-spec.md` and `design/ux-flows.md` are both still unreviewed by Serge/Aviran at `design-review` — flag both together, they're now interdependent.
